By (
2.62
),
V\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}(B_{1}\setminus\overline{B}_{\lambda_{1}})=\mathbf{var}\,(C,\theta_{C})\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}(B_{1}\setminus\overline{B}_{\lambda_{1}})
for some locally
\mathcal{H}^{n}
-rectifiable cone
C\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
and zero homogeneous
\theta_{C}:C\to\mathbb{N}
. Now since the integer rectifiable varifold cone
\mathbf{var}\,(C,\theta_{C})
is stationary in
B_{1}\setminus\overline{B}_{\lambda_{1}}
, it is stationary in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
by
n\geq 2
, and due to (
2.62
), satisfies
\int_{C\cap B_{1}}\theta_{C}\,d\mathcal{H}^{n}=\omega_{n}
. Therefore
C=K
for some
K\in\mathcal{H}
, and
\theta_{C}\equiv 1
. From the definition of
C
, it follows that

\mathrm{spt}\,V\cap(B_{1}\setminus\overline{B}_{\lambda_{1}})\subset K\,.

(2.63)

Finally, (
2.62
) and (
2.63
) give (
2.55
) with
\beta_{1}=\lambda_{1}
,
\beta_{2}=1
. The result of step one then completes the proof that
V=\mathbf{var}\,(K\cap B_{1},\left.1\right|_{K\cap B_{1}})
.

∎

Proof of Theorem
2.1
.

The proof proceeds in four steps, which we outline here. Precise statements can be found at the beginning of each step. First, we assume that
\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}(s/8)\geq 0
, and prove that
C^{1}
-graphicality can be propagated from
s/32
to an upper radius
S_{+}/16\leq S_{*}/16
as long as
\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}(S_{+})
remains non-negative and
S_{+}\leq\varepsilon_{0}/\Lambda
. This is then enough to prove the exterior blow-down result in part (ii) of Theorem
2.1
in step two. In the third step, we argue that if
\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}(s/8)\leq 0
, then
C^{1}
-graphicality can be propagated inwards from
S_{*}/2
down to
s/32
. The details in this step are quite similar to the first, so we summarize them. Finally, the first and third steps are combined in step four to conclude the proof Theorem
2.1
-(i), in which there are no sign restrictions on the deficit.

Step one
: In this step, given
n\geq 2
,
\Gamma\geq 0
, and
\sigma>0
, we prove the existence of
\varepsilon_{0}
and
M_{0}
(specified below in (
2.73
) and (
2.74
), and depending on
n
,
\Gamma
, and
\sigma
) such that if (
2.3
), (
2.4
), (
2.5
) and (
2.6
) hold with
\varepsilon_{0}
and
M_{0}
, and in addition,

0\leq\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}(s/8)\leq\varepsilon_{0}\,,

(2.64)

then there exists
K_{+}\in\mathcal{H}
and
u_{+}\in\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}(\Sigma_{K_{+}},s/32,S_{+}/16)
such that

V
corresponds to
\Sigma_{K_{+}}(u_{+},s/32,S_{+}/16)
on
A_{s/32}^{S_{+}/16}
,

(2.65)

where

R_{+}=\max\Big\{\sup\Big\{\rho\geq\frac{s}{8}:\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}(\rho)\geq 0\Big\},4\,s\Big\}\,,\quad S_{+}=\min\Big\{R_{+},\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{\Lambda}\Big\}\geq 4\,s\,.

(2.66)

We start by imposing some constraints on the constants
\varepsilon_{0}
and
M_{0}
. For the finite set

J=\Big\{\Big(\frac{1}{3},\frac{1}{6}\Big),\Big(\frac{2}{3},\frac{1}{3}\Big)\Big\}\subset\big\{(\eta_{0},\eta):\eta_{0}>\eta>0\big\}\,,

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\max\Big\{M_{1}\Big(n,\frac{\sigma}{2\,C_{0}},\Gamma,\Big(\frac{1}{8},\frac{1}{2}\Big),\Big(\frac{1}{6},\frac{1}{4}\Big),\Big(\frac{1}{32},\frac{1}{2}\Big)\Big),

(2.69)

\displaystyle\qquad M_{1}\Big(n,\frac{\sigma}{2\,C_{0}},\Gamma,\Big(\frac{1}{16},\frac{1}{8}\Big),\Big(\frac{3}{32},\frac{7}{64}\Big),\Big(\frac{1}{32},\frac{1}{2}\Big)\Big)\Big\}\,,

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\min\Big\{\varepsilon_{1}\Big(n,\frac{\sigma}{2\,C_{0}},\Gamma,\Big(\frac{1}{8},\frac{1}{2}\Big),\Big(\frac{1}{6},\frac{1}{4}\Big),\Big(\frac{1}{32},\frac{1}{2}\Big)\Big),

(2.70)

\displaystyle\qquad\varepsilon_{1}\Big(n,\frac{\sigma}{2\,C_{0}},\Gamma,\Big(\frac{1}{16},\frac{1}{8}\Big),\Big(\frac{3}{32},\frac{7}{64}\Big),\Big(\frac{1}{32},\frac{1}{2}\Big)\Big)\Big\}\,.

We also assume that

M_{0}\geq\max\Big\{M_{0}^{\prime}\,,M_{2}\Big(n,\overline{\sigma},\Gamma,\frac{1}{8},\Big(\frac{1}{32},\frac{1}{2}\Big)\Big)\Big\}\,.

Let us now recall that, by assumption,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{n}}\,\int_{A_{\lambda_{1}\,r}^{\lambda_{2}\,r}}\,\omega_{H_{\ell+1}}^{2}\,d\|V\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C(n)}{s_{\ell+1}^{n}}\,\int_{s_{\ell}}^{s_{\ell+1}}\,r^{n-1}\,dr\,\int_{\Sigma_{H_{\ell+1}}}[u_{\ell+1}]_{r}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(n)\,T_{\ell}+\frac{C(n)}{s_{\ell+1}^{n}}\,\int_{s_{\ell}}^{s_{\ell+1}}\,r^{n-1}\,dr\,\int_{\Sigma_{H_{\ell}}}[u_{\ell}]_{r}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(n)\,T_{\ell}\leq C(n)\,\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}\,,

where in the last inequality we have used (
2.88
)
j=ℓ
. Again by our choice (
2.70
) of
\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime}
, we deduce that (
2.52
) holds with
r
,
\lambda_{1}
and
\lambda_{2}
as in (
2.99
).
We can thus apply Lemma
2.8
-(i), and find
v\in\mathcal{X}_{\sigma/2\,C_{0}}(\Sigma_{H_{\ell+1}},s_{\ell+1}/4,4\,s_{\ell+1})
such that

\mbox{$V$ corresponds to $\Sigma_{H_{\ell+1}}(v,s_{\ell+1}/4,4\,s_{\ell+1})$ on $A_{s_{\ell+1}/4}^{4\,s_{\ell+1}}$}\,.

(2.100)

By (
2.95
), (
2.86
)
j=ℓ
, and (
2.100
),
v=u_{\ell+1}
on
\Sigma_{H_{\ell+1}}\times(s_{\ell+1}/4,4\,s_{\ell})
. We can thus use

to extend
u_{\ell+1}
from
\Sigma_{H_{\ell+1}}\times(s/32,4\,s_{\ell})
to
\Sigma_{H_{\ell+1}}\times(s/32,4\,s_{\ell+1})
, and, thanks to (

), (
2.86
)

and (

), the resulting extension is such that

u_{\ell+1}\in\mathcal{X}_{\sigma/2\,C_{0}}(\Sigma_{H_{\ell+1}},s_{\ell+1}/4,4\,s_{\ell+1})

and

(2.101)

\displaystyle\textup{$V$ corresponds to $\Sigma_{H_{\ell+1}}(u_{\ell+1},s/32,4\,s_{\ell+1})$ on $A_{s/32}^{4\,s_{\ell+1}}$}\,.

(2.102)

The bound (
2.101
) is part of (
2.85
)
j=ℓ+1
, and (
2.102
) is (
2.86
)
j=ℓ+1
, so in order to complete the proof of (

)

and (
2.86
)
j=ℓ+1
, it remains to show that the

-norm of

is bounded by
\sigma
in between

and

.

Towards this end, we record the following consequence of taking square roots in (
2.90
)
j=m
(using
\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}\geq 0
from (
2.84
)) and summing over
m=1,...,i
for any
1\leq i\leq\ell
: for
\alpha=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{-k/2}
and
\tilde{C}(n,\Gamma)=\tau^{1/2}(1+\Gamma)
,

\displaystyle S_{i}:=\sum_{m=0}^{i}\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}(s_{m})^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tau^{1/2}\sum_{m=0}^{i-1}\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}(s_{m})^{1/2}+(1+\Gamma)(\Lambda\,s_{m})^{1/2}

(2.103)

\displaystyle\qquad+\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}(s_{0})^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tau^{1/2}S_{i-1}+\alpha\,\tilde{C}(n,\Gamma)(\Lambda\,s_{i-1})^{1/2}+\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}(s_{0})^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tau^{1/2}S_{i-1}+(1+\alpha\,\tilde{C}(n,\Gamma))(\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime})^{1/2}\,,

where in the last line we have used (
2.84
) and (
2.80
). By induction, utilizing (
2.64
), (
2.73
) for the base case and (
2.103
) for the induction step we have

S_{i}\leq\frac{(1+\alpha\,\tilde{C}(n,\Gamma))(\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime})^{1/2}}{1-\tau^{1/2}}\quad\forall\,0\leq i\leq\ell\,.

(2.104)

Now by the positivity of
\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}
and (
2.91
)
j=ℓ
, for all
m=1,...,\ell
,

\displaystyle T_{m}^{1/2}\leq C(n)\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}(s_{m-1})^{1/2}+C(n)(\Lambda\,s_{m-1})^{1/2}\,,

(2.105)

In turn, by (
2.89
)
j=ℓ+1
, (
2.83
) and (
2.105
), then (
2.84
) and (
2.104
)
i=ℓ-1
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{C(n)}\sum_{m=1}^{\ell+1}|\nu_{H_{m}}-\nu_{H_{m-1}}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{\ell}T_{m}^{1/2}

(2.106)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime})^{1/2}+C(n)S_{\ell-1}+\alpha\,C(n)(\Lambda\,{s_{\ell-1}})^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(n,\Gamma)(\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime})^{1/2}

for suitable
C(n,\Gamma)
. Combining constants, we use (
2.106
) to see

\displaystyle|\nu_{H_{i}}-\nu_{H_{\ell+1}}|\leq C(n,\Gamma)(\varepsilon_{0}^{\prime})^{1/2}\quad\forall i=0,...,\ell\,.

(2.107)

Now
u_{i}\in\mathcal{X}_{\sigma/2\,C_{0}}(\Sigma_{H_{j}},s_{i}/4,4\,s_{i})
by (
2.85
)
j=i
, and
\sigma/2\,C_{0}
and
|\nu_{H_{i}}-\nu_{H_{\ell+1}}|
are small enough to apply Lemma

-(i) by our choice of
\sigma
above (
2.68
) and (
2.107
) with (
2.71
), respectively. Then we obtain

corresponding to

on

and in

, and by (

), (

),

\frac{\sigma}{2}+C_{0}\,|\nu_{H_{i}}-\nu_{H_{\ell+1}}|\leq\frac{\sigma}{2}+C_{0}\,\frac{\sigma}{2\,C_{0}}=\sigma\,,

so

. Finally, since they represent the same surface over

,

on
A^{4\,s_{i}}_{s_{i}/4}
. Gathering these estimates for

, which finishes the proof of (
2.85
)
j=ℓ+1
.

To prove (
2.90
)
j=ℓ+1
: We set
r_{0}=(s_{\ell}+s_{\ell+1})/2
and notice that for
\eta_{0}=1/3
,

r_{1}=r_{0}\,(1-\eta_{0})=s_{\ell}\,,\qquad r_{2}=r_{0}\,(1+\eta_{0})=s_{\ell+1}\,.

(2.108)

For
\eta=1/6
we correspondingly set

r_{3}=r_{0}\,(1-\eta)=:s_{\ell}^{-}\,,\qquad r_{4}=r_{0}\,(1+\eta)=:s_{\ell}^{+}\,,

(2.109)

and notice that
(\eta_{0},\eta)\in J
, see (
2.67
). With the aim of applying Theorem
2.7
-(iii) to these radii, we notice that (
2.86
)
j=ℓ+1
implies that assumption (
2.43
) holds with
H=H_{\ell+1}
and
u=u_{\ell+1}
, while, by (
2.96
),
r=s_{\ell}^{*}\in(s_{\ell},s_{\ell+1})
is such that (
2.45
) holds. By
\Lambda\,s_{\ell+1}\leq\varepsilon_{0}\leq 1
, (
2.84
), and (
2.46
), with
C(n)=C_{0}(n,1/6,1/3)
for
C_{0}
as in Theorem
2.7
-(iii), we have

\displaystyle s_{\ell+1}^{-n}\,\,\big|\|V\|\big(B_{s_{\ell}^{+}}\setminus B_{s_{\ell}^{-}}\big)-\omega_{n}\,\big((s_{\ell}^{+})^{n}-(s_{\ell}^{-})^{n}\big)\big|

\displaystyle=s_{\ell+1}^{-n}\big|\mathcal{H}^{n}(\Sigma_{H_{\ell+1}}(u_{\ell+1},s_{\ell}^{-},s_{\ell}^{+}))-\mathcal{H}^{n}(\Sigma_{H_{\ell+1}}(0,s_{\ell}^{-},s_{\ell}^{+}))\big|

\displaystyle\leq C(n)\,\big\{(\Lambda\,s_{\ell+1})^{2}\,\,+\Theta_{V,R,\Lambda}(s_{\ell+1})-\Theta_{V,R,\Lambda}(s_{\ell})\big\}\,.

(2.110)

Setting for brevity
\delta=\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}
and
\Theta=\Theta_{V,R,\Lambda}
, and recalling that

\displaystyle r^{n}\,\delta(r)=\omega_{n}\,r^{n}-\Theta(r)\,r^{n}

\displaystyle=\omega_{n}\,r^{n}-\|V\|(B_{r}\setminus B_{R})-\Lambda\,r^{n}\,\int_{R}^{r}\,\frac{\|V\|(B_{\rho}\setminus B_{R})}{\rho^{n}}\,d\rho+\frac{R\,\|\delta V\|(\partial B_{R})}{n}

we have

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!s_{\ell}^{-n}\,\big|(s_{\ell}^{-})^{n}\,\delta(s_{\ell}^{-})-(s_{\ell}^{+})^{n}\,\delta(s_{\ell}^{+})\big|\leq C(n)\,\big\{(\Lambda\,s_{\ell})^{2}+\Theta(s_{\ell+1})-\Theta(s_{\ell})\big\}

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!+C(n)\,\Lambda\,s_{\ell}^{-n}\Big\{(s_{\ell}^{+})^{n}\int_{R}^{s_{\ell}^{+}}\frac{\|V\|(B_{\rho}\setminus B_{R})}{\rho^{n}}\,d\rho-(s_{\ell}^{-})^{n}\int_{R}^{s_{\ell}^{-}}\frac{\|V\|(B_{\rho}\setminus B_{R})}{\rho^{n}}\,d\rho\Big\}

\displaystyle\!\!\leq C(n)\,\Big\{(\Lambda\,s_{\ell})^{2}+\Theta(s_{\ell+1})-\Theta(s_{\ell})\Big\}+C(n)\,\Lambda\,\int_{R}^{s_{\ell}^{+}}\frac{\|V\|(B_{\rho}\setminus B_{R})}{\rho^{n}}\,d\rho\,.

By
\Lambda\,s_{\ell}\leq 1
and since
s_{\ell}^{+}\leq s_{\ell}\leq\varepsilon_{0}/8\,\Lambda
thanks to
\ell<N
, we can use the upper bound
\|V\|(B_{\rho}\setminus B_{R})\leq\Gamma\,\rho^{n}
with
\rho\in(R,s_{\ell}^{+})\subset(R,1/\Lambda)
, to find that

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{(s_{\ell}^{-})^{n}}{s_{\ell}^{n}}\,\delta(s_{\ell}^{-})-\frac{(s_{\ell}^{+})^{n}}{s_{\ell}^{n}}\,\delta(s_{\ell}^{+})\Big|\leq\!C_{*}(n)\,\big\{\delta(s_{\ell})-\delta(s_{\ell+1})\big\}+C_{*}(n)\,(\Gamma+1)\,\Lambda\,s_{\ell}\,,

for a constant
C_{*}(n)
. By rearranging terms and using the monotonicity of
\delta
on
(R,\infty)
and
(s_{\ell}^{-},s_{\ell}^{+})\subset(s_{\ell},s_{\ell+1})
we find that

\displaystyle\big(C_{*}(n)\,+(s_{\ell}^{+})^{n}/(s_{\ell}^{n})\big)\,\delta(s_{\ell+1})\leq C_{*}(n)\,\delta(s_{\ell+1})+\big((s_{\ell}^{+})^{n}/(s_{\ell}^{n})\big)\,\delta(s_{\ell}^{+})

(2.111)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{*}(n)\,\delta(s_{\ell})+\big((s_{\ell}^{-})^{n}/(s_{\ell}^{n})\big)\,\delta(s_{\ell}^{-})+C_{*}(n)\,(1+\Gamma)\,\Lambda\,s_{\ell}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\big(C_{*}(n)\,+(s_{\ell}^{-})^{n}/(s_{\ell}^{n})\big)\,\delta(s_{\ell})+C_{*}(n)\,(1+\Gamma)\,\Lambda\,s_{\ell}\,.

We finally notice that by (
2.108
), (
2.109
),
\eta_{0}=1/3
, and
\eta=1/6
, we have

\frac{s_{\ell}^{-}}{s_{\ell}}=\frac{r_{0}\,(1-\eta)}{r_{0}\,(1-\eta_{0})}=\frac{5}{4}\,,\qquad\frac{s_{\ell}^{+}}{s_{\ell}}=2\,\frac{s_{\ell}^{+}}{s_{\ell+1}}=2\,\frac{1+\eta}{1+\eta_{0}}=\frac{7}{4}\,,

so that, we find
\delta(s_{\ell+1})\leq\tau\{\delta(s_{\ell})+(1+\Gamma)\,\Lambda\,s_{\ell}\}
(i.e. (
2.90
)
j=ℓ+1
) with

\tau=\tau(n)=\frac{C_{*}(n)+(5/4)^{n}}{C_{*}(n)+(7/4)^{n}}\,,\qquad\tau_{*}=\tau_{*}(n)=\frac{C_{*}(n)}{C_{*}(n)+(7/4)^{n}}<\tau\,.

To prove (
2.91
)
j=ℓ+1
: We finally prove (
2.91
)
j=ℓ+1
, i.e.

\frac{1}{s_{\ell+1}^{n}}\int_{s_{\ell+1}}^{2\,s_{\ell+1}}\!\!\!r^{n-1}\!\!\int_{\Sigma_{H_{\ell+1}}}\!\!\!\!\![u_{\ell+1}]_{r}^{2}\leq C(n)\big\{\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}(s_{\ell})-\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}(s_{\ell+3})+\Lambda\,s_{\ell}\big\}.

(2.112)

By (
2.86
)
j=ℓ+1
we know that

\mbox{$V$ corresponds to $\Sigma_{H_{\ell+1}}(u_{\ell+1},s/32,4\,s_{\ell+1})$ on $A_{s/32}^{4\,s_{\ell+1}}$}\,.

(2.113)

Now, (
2.41
) holds with
r_{0}=3\,s_{\ell}
and
(\eta_{0},\eta)=(2/3,1/3)\in J
, see (
2.67
), if

\displaystyle r_{1}=s_{\ell}=3\,s_{\ell}-2\,s_{\ell}\,,\qquad r_{2}=5\,s_{\ell}=3\,s_{\ell}+2\,s_{\ell}\,,

\displaystyle r_{3}=s_{\ell+1}=3\,s_{\ell}-s_{\ell}\,,\qquad\,\,\,\,\,r_{4}=2\,s_{\ell+1}=3\,s_{\ell}+s_{\ell}\,.

Since
s_{\ell}^{*}\in(s_{\ell},s_{\ell+1})\subset(r_{1},r_{2})
,
by (
2.113
), (
2.96
) and
(r_{1},r_{2})\subset(s/32,4\,s_{\ell+1})
we can apply Theorem
2.6
to deduce that

\int_{s_{\ell+1}}^{2\,s_{\ell+1}}\!\!\!r^{n-1}\!\int_{\Sigma_{H_{\ell+1}}}\,[u_{\ell+1}]_{r}^{2}\leq C(n)\,\int_{s_{\ell}}^{5\,s_{\ell}}\!\!\!\!r^{n+1}\!\int_{\Sigma_{H_{\ell+1}}}\!\!\!\!\!\!(\partial_{r}u_{\ell+1})_{r}^{2}+C(n)\,\Lambda\,(s_{\ell})^{n+1}\,.

Again by (
2.113
), Theorem
2.7
-(ii) with
(r_{1},r_{2})=(s_{\ell},8\,s_{\ell})
gives

\displaystyle s_{\ell}^{-n}\,\int_{s_{\ell}}^{5\,s_{\ell}}\,r^{n+1}\,\int_{\Sigma_{H_{\ell+1}}}\,(\partial_{r}[u_{\ell+1}])_{r}^{2}\leq s_{\ell}^{-n}\,\int_{s_{\ell}}^{8\,s_{\ell}}\,r^{n+1}\,\int_{\Sigma_{H_{\ell+1}}}\,(\partial_{r}[u_{\ell+1}])_{r}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(n)\,\big\{\Theta_{V,R,\Lambda}(8\,s_{\ell})-\Theta_{V,R,\Lambda}(s_{\ell})\big\}\leq C(n)\,\big\{\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}(s_{\ell})-\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}(s_{\ell+3})\big\}\,.

The last two estimates combined give (
2.112
), which finishes the
claim
.

Proof of (
2.65
)
: We assume
S_{+}<\infty
(that is either
\Lambda>0
or
R_{+}<\infty
), and recall that we have already proved (
2.65
) if
S_{+}=4\,s
. Otherwise,
N
(as defined in (
2.84
)) is finite, with
2^{N}\leq\frac{S_{+}}{s}<2^{N+1}
. By (
2.85
)
j=N-2
and (
2.86
)
j=N-2
, we have that
u_{N-2}\in\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}(\Sigma_{H_{N-2}},s/32,4\,s_{N-2})
and
V
corresponds to
\Sigma_{H_{N-2}}(u_{N-2},s/32,4\,s_{N-2})
on
A_{s/32}^{4\,s_{N-2}}
. Since
4\,s_{N-2}=2^{N+1}\,s/16>S_{+}/16
, we deduce (
2.65
) with
K_{+}=H_{N-2}
and
u_{+}=u_{N-2}
.

Step two
: In this step we prove statement (ii) in Theorem
2.1
. We assume that
\Lambda=0
and that

\delta(r)\geq-\varepsilon_{0}\qquad\forall r\geq\frac{s}{8}\,,

(2.114)

where we have set for brevity
\delta=\delta_{V,R,0}
. We must first show that

\delta(r)\geq 0\quad\forall r\geq\frac{s}{8}\,.

(2.115)

Since
\delta
is decreasing in
r
, it has a limit
\lim_{r\to\infty}\delta(r)=:\delta_{\infty}\geq-\varepsilon_{0}
. Next, we know that for any sequence
R_{i}\to\infty
,
V/R_{i}
converges locally in the varifold sense to a limiting integer rectifiable varifold cone
W
. By the local varifold convergence and
n\geq 2
,
W
is stationary in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
, and it is the case that

\delta_{W,0,0}(r)=\delta_{\infty}\geq-\varepsilon_{0}\quad\forall r>0\,.

Up to decreasing
\varepsilon_{0}
if necessary (and recalling that
\delta_{W,0,0}
is the usual area excess multiplied by
-1
), Allard’s theorem and the fact that
W/r=W
imply that
W
corresponds to a multiplicity one plane. In particular, it must be that
\delta_{\infty}=0
, which together with the monotonicity of
\delta
yields (
2.115
).

By (
2.115
),
S_{+}=S_{*}=\infty
, and so by (
2.85
) and (
2.86
), there is a sequence
\{(H_{j},u_{j})\}_{j=0}^{N}
but with
N=\infty
now, satisfying

\displaystyle\mbox{$V$ corresponds to $\Sigma_{H_{j}}(u_{j},s/32,4\,s_{j})$ on $A_{s/32}^{4\,s_{j}}$}\quad\forall j\geq 0\,,

(2.116)

\displaystyle|\nu_{H_{j}}-\nu_{H_{j-1}}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(n)\,T_{j-1}\,,\qquad\hskip 22.76228pt\mbox{if $j\geq 1$}\,,

(2.117)

\displaystyle\delta(s_{j})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{split}&\varepsilon_{0}\,,\hskip 49.79231pt\qquad\mbox{if $j=0$}\,,\\
&\tau\,\delta(s_{j-1})\,,\hskip 17.92537pt\qquad\mbox{if $j\geq 1$}\,,\end{split}\right.

(2.118)

\displaystyle T_{j}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{split}&C(n)\,\varepsilon_{0}\,,\hskip 24.18501pt\qquad\mbox{if $j=0$}\,,\\
&C(n)\,\delta(s_{j-1})\,,\qquad\mbox{if $j\geq 1$}\,.\end{split}\right.

(2.119)

Notice that, in asserting the validity of (
2.119
) with
j\geq 1
, we have used (
2.115
) to estimate
-\delta(s_{j+2})\leq 0
in (
2.91
)
j
. By iterating (
2.118
) we find

\delta(s_{j})\leq\tau^{j}\,\delta(s/8)\leq\tau^{j}\,\varepsilon_{0}\,,\qquad\forall j\geq 1\,,

(2.120)

which, combined with (
2.119
) and (
2.117
), gives, for every
j\geq 1
,

\displaystyle T_{j}\leq C(n)\,\min\{1,\tau^{j-1}\}\,\delta(s/8)\leq C(n)\,\tau^{j}\,\delta(s/8)\,,

(2.121)

\displaystyle|\nu_{H_{j}}-\nu_{H_{j-1}}|^{2}\leq C(n)\,\min\{1,\tau^{j-2}\}\,\delta(s/8)\leq C(n)\,\tau^{j}\,\delta(s/8)\,,

(2.122)

thanks also to
\tau=\tau(n)
and, again, to (
2.115
). By (
2.122
), for every
j\geq 0
,
k\geq 1
, we have
|\nu_{H_{j+k}}-\nu_{H_{j}}|\leq C(n)\,\sqrt{\delta(s/8)}\,\sum_{h=1}^{k+1}\big(\sqrt{\tau}\big)^{j-1+h}
, so that there exists
K\in\mathcal{H}
such that

\varepsilon_{j}^{2}:=|\nu_{K}-\nu_{H_{j}}|^{2}\leq C(n)\,\tau^{j}\,\delta(s/8)\,,\qquad\forall j\geq 1\,,

(2.123)

In particular, for
j
large enough, we have
\varepsilon_{j}<\varepsilon_{0}
, and thus, by Lemma
2.5
-(i) and by (
2.116
) we can find
v_{j}\in\mathcal{X}_{C(n)\,(\sigma+\varepsilon_{j})}(\Sigma_{K};s/32,4\,s_{j})
such that

\mbox{$V$ corresponds to $\Sigma_{K}(v_{j},s/32,4\,s_{j})$ on $A_{s/32}^{4\,s_{j}}$}\,.

(2.124)

By (
2.124
),
v_{j+1}=v_{j}
on
\Sigma_{K}\times(s/32,4\,s_{j})
. Since
s_{j}\to\infty
we have thus found
u\in\mathcal{X}_{C(n)\,\sigma}(\Sigma_{K};s/32,\infty)
such that

\mbox{$V$ corresponds to $\Sigma_{K}(u,s/32,\infty)$ on $A_{s/32}^{\infty}$}\,,

(2.125)

which is (
2.7
) with
S_{*}=\infty
.

To prove (
2.8
), we notice that if
r\in(s_{j},s_{j+1})
for some
j\geq 1
, then, setting
\tau=(1/2)^{\alpha}
(i.e.,
\alpha=\log_{1/2}(\tau)\in(0,1)
) and noticing that
r/s\leq 2^{j+1-3}
, by (
2.76
) and (
2.120
) we have

\displaystyle\delta(r)\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\!\!\!\delta(s_{j})\leq\tau^{j}\,\delta(s/8)=2^{-j\,a}\,\delta(s/8)=4^{-\alpha}\,2^{-(j-2)\alpha}\,\delta(s/8)

(2.126)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\!\!\!C(n)\,(s/r)^{\alpha}\,\delta(s/8)\,,\,\,\,\,\,

where in the last inequality (
2.114
) was used again; this proves (
2.8
). To prove (
2.9
), we recall that
\omega_{K}(y)=\arctan(|\nu_{K}\cdot\hat{y}|/|\mathbf{p}_{K}\,\hat{y}|)
, provided
\arctan
is defined on
\mathbb{R}\cup\{\pm\infty\}
, and where
\hat{y}=y/|y|
,
y\neq 0
. Now, by (
2.125
),

y=|y|\,\frac{\mathbf{p}_{K}\,\hat{y}+u(\mathbf{p}_{K}\,\hat{y},|y|)\,\nu_{K}}{\sqrt{1+u(\mathbf{p}_{K}\,\hat{y},|y|)^{2}}}\,,\qquad\forall y\in({\rm spt}\,V)\setminus B_{s/32}\,,

so that
|\mathbf{p}_{K}\,\hat{y}|\geq 1/2
for
y\in({\rm spt}V)\setminus B_{s/32}
; therefore, by (
2.123
), up to further decreasing the value of
\varepsilon_{0}
, and recalling
\delta(s/8)\leq\varepsilon_{0}
, we conclude

|\mathbf{p}_{H_{j}}\,\hat{y}|\geq\frac{1}{3}\,,\qquad\forall y\in({\rm spt}V)\setminus B_{s/32}\,,

(2.127)

for every
j\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{+\infty\}
(if we set
H_{\infty}=K
). By (
2.127
) we easily find

|\omega_{K}(y)-\omega_{H_{j}}(y)|\leq C\,|\nu_{H_{j}}-\nu_{K}|\,,\qquad\forall y\in({\rm spt}V)\setminus B_{s/32}\,,\forall j\geq 1\,,

from which we deduce that, if
j\geq 1
and
r\in(s_{j},s_{j+1})
, then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{n}}\,\int_{A_{r}^{2\,r}}\omega_{K}^{2}\,d\|V\|\!\!\!

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\!\!\!C(n)\,\Big\{\frac{1}{s_{j}^{n}}\,\int_{A_{s_{j}}^{s_{j+1}}}\omega_{K}^{2}\,d\|V\|+\frac{1}{s_{j+1}^{n}}\,\int_{A_{s_{j+1}}^{s_{j+2}}}\omega_{K}^{2}\,d\|V\|\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\!\!\!C(n)\,\Big\{\frac{1}{s_{j}^{n}}\,\int_{A_{s_{j}}^{s_{j+1}}}\omega_{H_{j}}^{2}\,d\|V\|+\frac{1}{s_{j+1}^{n}}\,\int_{A_{s_{j+1}}^{s_{j+2}}}\omega_{H_{j+1}}^{2}\,d\|V\|\Big\}

\displaystyle\!\!\!+C(n)\,\Gamma\,\big\{|\nu_{K}-\nu_{H_{j}}|^{2}+|\nu_{K}-\nu_{H_{j+1}}|^{2}\big\}\,,

where (
2.75
) was used to bound
\|V\|(A_{\rho}^{2\,\rho})\leq\Gamma\,(2\,\rho)^{n}
with
\rho=s_{j},s_{j+1}\in(R,1/\Lambda)
.
By (
2.116
) we can exploit (
2.30
) on the first two integrals, so that taking (
2.123
) into account we find that, if
j\geq 1
and
r\in(s_{j},s_{j+1})
, then
r^{-n}\,\int_{A_{r}^{2\,r}}\omega_{K}^{2}\,d\|V\|\leq C(n)\{T_{j}+T_{j+1}\big\}+C(n)\,\Gamma\,\tau^{j}\,\delta(s/8)\leq C(n)\,(1+\Gamma)\,\tau^{j}\,\delta(s/8)
, where in the last inequality we have used (
2.121
). Since
\tau^{j}\leq C(n)\,(s/r)^{\alpha}
, we conclude the proof of (
2.9
), and thus, of Theorem
2.1
-(ii).

Step three
: In this step, given
n\geq 2
,
\Gamma\geq 0
, and
\sigma>0
, we claim the existence of
\varepsilon_{0}
and
M_{0}
, depending only on
n
,
\Gamma
, and
\sigma
, such that if (
2.3
), (
2.4
), (
2.5
) and (
2.6
) hold with
\varepsilon_{0}
and
M_{0}
, and in addition,

-\varepsilon_{0}\leq\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}(s/8)\leq 0\,,

(2.128)

then there exists
K_{-}\in\mathcal{H}
and
u_{-}\in\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}(\Sigma_{K_{-}},s/32,S_{*}/2)
such that

V
corresponds to
\Sigma_{K_{-}}(u_{+},s/32,S_{*}/2)
on
A_{s/32}^{S_{*}/2}
,

(2.129)

where
S_{*}
and
R_{*}
are as in Theorem
2.1
.
The argument is quite similar to that of the first step, with minor differences due to the opposite sign of the deficit. The first is that the iteration instead begins at the outer radius
S_{*}
and proceeds inwards via intermediate radii
s_{j}=2^{-j}S_{*}
, and the second is that, in the analogue of the graphicality propagation claims (
2.85
)
j=ℓ+1
and (
2.86
)
j=ℓ+1
, the negative sign on
\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}
is used to sum the “tilting” between successive planes
H_{j}
and
H_{j+1}
.

Step four
: Finally, we combine steps one and three to prove statement (i) in Theorem
2.1
. Before choosing the parameters
\varepsilon_{0}
and
M_{0}
, we need a preliminary result. We claim that for any
\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
, there exists
\sigma^{\prime}(\varepsilon^{\prime})>0
such that if
r_{1}<r_{2}
,
K_{1}
,
K_{2}\in\mathcal{H}
with
\nu_{K_{1}}\cdot\nu_{K_{2}}\geq 0
and accompanying
u_{i}\in\mathcal{X}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(\Sigma_{K_{i}},r_{1},r_{2})
, and
M
is a smooth hypersurface such that
M\cap A_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}
corresponds to
\Sigma_{K_{i}}(u_{i},r_{1},r_{2})
for
k=1,2
, then

\displaystyle|\nu_{{K_{1}}}-\nu_{{K_{2}}}|<\varepsilon^{\prime}\,.

(2.130)

This is immediate from
\nu_{K_{1}}\cdot\nu_{K_{2}}\geq 0
the fact that the
L^{\infty}
-bounds on
u_{i}
imply that
M
is contained in the intersection of two cones containing
K_{1}
and
K_{2}
, whose openings become arbitrarily narrow as
\sigma^{\prime}\to 0
.

Fix
n\geq 2
,
\Gamma\geq 0
, and
\sigma>0
; we assume without loss of generality that
\sigma<\sigma_{0}
, where
\sigma_{0}
is the dimension-dependent constant from Lemma
2.5
. We choose
\varepsilon^{\prime}
with corresponding
\sigma^{\prime}
according to (
2.130
) such that, up to decreasing
\sigma^{\prime}
if necessary,

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\prime}<\varepsilon_{0}\,,\quad C_{0}(\sigma^{\prime}+\varepsilon^{\prime})\leq\sigma\,,

(2.131)

where
\varepsilon_{0}
,
C_{0}
are as in Lemma
2.5
. Next, we choose
\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon_{0}(n,\Gamma,\sigma)
and
M_{0}=M_{0}(n,\Gamma,\sigma)
to satisfy several restrictions: first,
\varepsilon_{0}
is smaller than the
\varepsilon_{0}
from Lemma
2.5
and each
\varepsilon_{0}(n,\Gamma,\sigma^{\prime})
from steps one and three, and
M_{0}
is larger than
M_{0}(n,\Gamma,\sigma^{\prime})
from those steps; second, with
\varepsilon_{2}
and
M_{2}
as in Lemma
2.8
-(ii), we also assume that

\varepsilon_{0}\leq\min\Big\{\varepsilon^{\prime},\varepsilon_{2}\Big(n,\sigma^{\prime},\Gamma,\frac{1}{16},\Big(\frac{1}{128},\frac{1}{2}\Big)\Big)\Big\},\,M_{0}\geq M_{2}\Big(n,\sigma^{\prime},\Gamma,\frac{1}{16},\Big(\frac{1}{128},\frac{1}{2}\Big)\Big)

(2.132)

In the remainder of this step, we suppose that

\mbox{$V$ satisfies \eqref{Gamma bounds}, \eqref{mesoscale bounds}, \eqref{mesoscale delta small s8} and \eqref{mesoscale Rstar larger s4} at mesoscale $s$}\,.

(2.133)

In proving Theorem
2.1
-(i), there are three cases depending on whether
\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}
changes sign on
[s/8,S_{*}]
.

Case one
:
\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}(r)\geq 0
for all
r\in[s/8,S_{*}]
. If the deficit is non-negative, then in particular

0\leq\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}(s/8)\leq\varepsilon_{0}

(2.134)

and
S_{*}=S_{+}
, where
S_{+}
was defined in (
2.66
). By our choice of
\varepsilon_{0}
and

at the beginning of this step and the equivalence of (

) and (
2.64
), step one applies and the conclusion (

) is (
2.7
). Thus Theorem
2.1
-(i) is proved.

Case two
:
\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}(r)\leq 0
for all
r\in[s/8,S_{*}]
. Should the deficit be non-positive in this interval, then in particular, (
2.128
) holds in addition to (
2.3
), (
2.4
), (
2.5
) and (
2.6
). Therefore, by our choice of
\varepsilon_{0}
and
M_{0}
, step three applies. The conclusion (
2.129
) is (
2.7
) (in fact with larger upper radii
S_{*}/2
), and Theorem
2.1
-(i) is proved.

Case three
:
\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}
changes sign in
[s/8,S_{*}]
. By the monotonicity of
\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}
,

\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}(s/8)>0>\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}(S_{*})\,.

(2.135)

First, by (
2.135
), (
2.64
) is satisfied, so (
2.65
) gives
K_{+}\in\mathcal{H}
and
u_{+}\in\mathcal{X}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(\Sigma_{K_{+}},s/32,S_{+}/16)
such that

V
corresponds to
\Sigma_{K_{+}}(u_{+},s/32,S_{+}/16)
on
A_{s/32}^{S_{+}/16}
,

(2.136)

where

R_{+}=\max\Big\{\sup\Big\{\rho\geq\frac{s}{8}:\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}(\rho)\geq 0\Big\},4\,s\Big\}\,,\quad S_{+}=\min\Big\{R_{+},\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{\Lambda}\Big\}\,.

(2.137)

If
S_{+}=S_{*}
, then (
2.136
) is (
2.7
) and we are done. So we assume for the rest of this case that
S_{+}<S_{*}
, which implies
S_{+}\neq\varepsilon_{0}/\Lambda
and thus

4\,s\leq R_{+}=S_{+}<S_{*}\,.

(2.138)

Next, we make the following

Claim:
There exists
K_{-}\in\mathcal{H}
and
u_{-}\in\mathcal{X}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(\Sigma_{K_{-}},R_{+}/2,S_{*}/2)
such that

\textup{$V$ corresponds to $\Sigma_{K_{-}}(u_{-},R_{+}/2,S_{*}/2)$ on $A_{R_{+}/2}^{S_{*}/2}$}\,.

(2.139)

Proof of the claim:
There are two subcases.

Subcase one
:
16\,R_{+}<\varepsilon_{0}/4\,\Lambda
and
64\,R_{+}<R_{*}
. We claim the conditions of step three are verified at
s^{\prime}=16\,R_{+}
. First, (

) holds from (

), and

\max\{64,M_{0}\}\,R<16\,R_{+}<\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{4\,\Lambda}

(which is (
2.4
)) holds due to the assumption of the subcase and
16R_{+}\geq s>\max\{64,M_{0}\}R
. Next,
2\,R_{+}<R_{*}/4
by the assumption of the subcase, which combined with the monotonicity of
\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}
and (
2.137
) gives
-\varepsilon_{0}\leq\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}(2\,R_{+})\leq 0
. This implies (
2.128
) and (
2.5
) with
s^{\prime}=16\,R_{+}
. Lastly, (
2.6
) holds at
s^{\prime}=16\,R_{+}
since

. Thus we apply (
2.129
) at
s^{\prime}=16\,R_{+}
, finding (
2.139
).

Subcase two
: One or both of
16\,R_{+}\geq\varepsilon_{0}/4\,\Lambda
,
64\,R_{+}\geq R_{*}
hold. In this case,

64\,R_{+}\geq\min\{\varepsilon_{0}/\Lambda,R_{*}\}=S_{*}\,.

(2.140)

We wish to apply Lemma
2.8
-(ii) with
r=S_{*}
,
\lambda_{1}=\frac{1}{16}
,
(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})=(\frac{1}{128},\frac{1}{2})
. By (
2.133
), (
2.48
) holds for
V
, and by (
2.132
),
(
2.133
), and
S_{*}\geq 4\,s
,

\max\{M_{2},64\}\,R\leq s\leq\frac{S_{*}}{4}\leq S_{*}\leq\frac{\varepsilon_{2}}{\Lambda}\,,

which is (
2.53
). Finally, we have
R_{*}\geq S_{*}/16\geq s/8
, so that by the definition of
R_{*}
, (
2.5
), the monotonicity of
\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}
, and (
2.132
),

\max\Big\{\Big|\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}\Big(\frac{S_{*}}{16}\Big)\Big|,|\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}(S_{*})|\Big\}\leq\varepsilon_{0}\leq\varepsilon_{2}\,,

which is (
2.54
). By the choices (
2.132
), Lemma
2.8
-(ii) applies and yields the existence of
K_{-}\in\mathcal{H}
and
u_{-}\in\mathcal{X}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(\Sigma_{K_{-}},S_{*}/128,S_{*}/2)
such that

V
corresponds to
\Sigma_{K_{-}}(u_{+},S_{*}/128,S_{*}/2)
on
A_{S_{*}/128}^{S_{*}/2}
,

(2.141)

By (
2.140
),
S_{*}/128\leq R_{+}/2
, so (
2.141
) implies (
2.139
). The proof of the
claim
is complete.

Returning to the proof of Theorem
2.1
-(i) under the assumption (
2.135
), we recall (
2.138
) and choose
R^{\prime}\in(R_{+},\min\{2\,R_{+},S_{*}\})
. Again, we want to apply Lemma
2.8
-(ii), this time with
r=R^{\prime}
,
\lambda_{1}=\frac{1}{16}
, and
(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})=(1/128,1/2)
. To begin with,
V
satisfies (
2.48
) as usual from (
2.133
). Second, (
2.53
) holds at
R^{\prime}
by
s\leq R^{\prime}\leq S_{*}
, (
2.133
), and the choices (
2.132
). By the monotonicity of
\delta_{V,R,\Lambda}
and
[R^{\prime}/16,R^{\prime}]\subset[R_{+}/16,S_{*}]\subset[s/4,S_{*}]
, (
2.54
) is valid by our choice (
2.132
) of
\varepsilon_{0}
. The graphicality result from Lemma
2.8
-(ii) therefore yields
K\in\mathcal{H}
and
u\in\mathcal{X}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(\Sigma_{K},R^{\prime}/128,R^{\prime}/2)
such that

\mbox{$V$ corresponds to $\Sigma_{K}(u,R^{\prime}/128,R^{\prime}/2)$ on $A_{R^{\prime}/128}^{R^{\prime}/2}$}\,.

(2.142)

Now
s/32\leq R^{\prime}/128<R_{+}/64<S_{+}/16
by
R^{\prime}<2\,R_{+}
and (
2.138
), and
R_{+}/2<R^{\prime}/2<S_{*}/2
, so by (

) and (

), respectively, we have

V
corresponds to
\Sigma_{K_{+}}(u_{+},R^{\prime}/128,S_{+}/16)
on
A_{R^{\prime}/128}^{S_{+}/16}

(2.143)

\displaystyle\mbox{$V$ corresponds to $\Sigma_{K_{-}}(u_{-},R_{+}/2,R^{\prime}/2)$ on $A_{R_{+}/2}^{R^{\prime}/2}$}\,,

(2.144)

where
u_{+}\in\mathcal{X}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(\Sigma_{K_{+}},R^{\prime}/128,S_{+}/16)
and
u_{-}\in\mathcal{X}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(\Sigma_{K_{-}},R_{+}/2,R^{\prime}/2)
. Furthermore, up multiplying
\nu_{K_{+}}
or
\nu_{K_{-}}
by minus one, we may assume
\nu_{K}\cdot\nu_{K_{\pm}}\geq 0
. Thus
V
is represented by multiple spherical graphs on nontrivial annuli. By combining (
2.142
), (
2.143
) and (
2.144
),
\nu_{K}\cdot\nu_{K_{\pm}}\geq 0
and
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma^{\prime}(\varepsilon^{\prime})
, (
2.130
) applies and gives

|\nu_{K}-\nu_{K_{+}}|<\varepsilon^{\prime}\,,\quad|\nu_{K}-\nu_{K_{-}}|<\varepsilon^{\prime}\,.

(2.145)

But
\varepsilon^{\prime}
was chosen according to (
2.131
) so that Lemma
2.5
-(i) is applicable; that is, since
\varepsilon^{\prime}<\varepsilon_{0}
and
\sigma^{\prime}<\sigma_{0}
from that lemma, we may reparametrize (
