) and (
2.139
), respectively, as

V
corresponds to
\Sigma_{K}(w_{+},s/32,S_{+}/16)
on
A_{s/32}^{S_{+}/16}

(2.146)

\displaystyle\mbox{$V$ corresponds to $\Sigma_{K}(w_{-},R_{+}/2,S_{*}/2)$ on $A_{R_{+}/2}^{S_{*}/2}$}\,,

(2.147)

where

w_{+}\in\mathcal{X}_{C_{0}(\sigma^{\prime}+\varepsilon^{\prime})}(\Sigma_{K},s/32,S_{+}/16)\,,\quad w_{-}\in\mathcal{X}_{C_{0}(\sigma^{\prime}+\varepsilon^{\prime})}(\Sigma_{K},R_{+}/2,S_{*}/2)\,.

By (
2.131
),
C_{0}(\sigma^{\prime}+\varepsilon^{\prime})\leq\sigma
, and by
R^{\prime}/128<S_{+}/16<R_{+}/2<R^{\prime}/2
, (
2.146
) and (
2.147
), we may extend the
u
defined in (
2.142
) onto
\Sigma_{K}\times(s/32,S_{*}/2)
using
w_{+}
and
w_{-}
with
C^{1}
-norm bounded by
\sigma
. The resulting extension is such that (
2.7
) holds, so the proof of Theorem
2.1
is finished.

∎

3.
Application of quantitative isoperimetry

Here we apply quantitative isoperimetry to prove Theorem
1.6
-(i) and parts of Theorem
1.6
-(iv).

Theorem 3.1
.

If
W\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
is compact,
v>0
, then
{\rm Min}[\psi_{W}(v)]\neq\emptyset
. Moreover, depending on
n
and
W
only, there are
v_{0}
,
C_{0}
,
\Lambda_{0}
positive,
s_{0}\in(0,1)
, and
R_{0}(v)
with
R_{0}(v)\to 0^{+}
and
R_{0}(v)\,v^{1/(n+1)}\to\infty
as
v\to\infty
, such that, if
v>v_{0}
and
E_{v}
is a minimizer of
\psi_{W}(v)
, then:

(i):

E_{v}
is a
(\Lambda_{0}/v^{1/(n+1)},s_{0}\,v^{1/(n+1)})
-perimeter minimizer with free boundary in
\Omega
, that is

P(E_{v};\Omega\cap B_{r}(x))\leq P(F;\Omega\cap B_{r}(x))+\frac{\Lambda_{0}}{v^{1/(n+1)}}\,\big|E_{v}\Delta F\big|\,,

(3.1)

for every
F\subset\Omega=\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus W
with
E_{v}\Delta F\subset\subset B_{r}(x)
and
r<s_{0}\,v^{1/(n+1)}
;

(ii):

E_{v}
determines
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
such that

|E_{v}\Delta B^{(v)}(x)|\leq C_{0}\,v^{-1+1/[2(n+1)]}\,;

(3.2)

if
\mathcal{R}(W)>0
, then
E_{v}
also determines
u\in C^{\infty}(\partial B^{(1)})
such that

\displaystyle(\partial E_{v})\setminus B_{R_{0}\,v^{1/(n+1)}}

(3.3)

\displaystyle=\Big\{y+v^{1/(n+1)}u\Big(\frac{y-x}{v^{1/(n+1)}}\Big)\,\nu_{B^{(v)}(x)}(y):y\in\partial B^{(v)}(x)\Big\}\setminus B_{R_{0}\,v^{1/(n+1)}}\,;

(iii):
if
\mathcal{R}(W)>0
and
x
and
u
depend on
E_{v}
as in (
3.2
) and (
3.3
), then

\lim_{v\to\infty}\sup_{E_{v}\in{\rm Min}[\psi_{W}(v)]}\!\!\!\max\big\{\big||x|\,v^{-1/(n+1)}-\omega_{n+1}^{-1/(n+1)}\big|\,,\|u\|_{C^{1}(\partial B^{(1)})}\big\}=0\,.

(3.4)

Remark 3.2

(Improved convergence)
.

We will repeatedly use the following fact (see, e.g.
[
FM11
,
FFM+15
,
CL12
,
CLM16
]
):
If
\Omega
is an open set,
\Lambda\geq 0
,
s>0
, if
\{F_{j}\}_{j}
are
(\Lambda,s)
-perimeter minimizers in
\Omega
, i.e. if it holds that

P(F_{j};B_{r}(x))\leq P(G_{j};B_{r}(x))+\Lambda\,|F_{j}\Delta G_{j}|\,,

(3.5)

whenever
G_{j}\Delta F_{j}\subset\subset B_{r}(x)\subset\subset\Omega
and
r<s
, and if
F
is an open set with smooth boundary in
\Omega
such that
F_{j}\to F
in
L^{1}_{{\rm loc}}(\Omega)
as
j\to\infty
, then for every
\Omega^{\prime}\subset\subset\Omega
there is
j(\Omega^{\prime})
such that

(\partial F_{j})\cap\Omega^{\prime}=\Big\{y+u_{j}(y)\,\nu_{F}(y):y\in\Omega\cap\partial F\Big\}\cap\Omega^{\prime}\,,\qquad\forall j\geq j(\Omega^{\prime})\,,

for a sequence
\{u_{j}\}_{j}\subset C^{1}(\Omega\cap\partial F)
with
\|u_{j}\|_{C^{1}(\Omega\cap\partial F)}\to 0
.

Compare the terminology used in (
3.1
) and (
3.5
): when we add “with free boundary”, the “localizing balls”
B_{r}(x)
are not required to be compactly contained in
\Omega
, and the perimeters are computed in
B_{r}(x)\cap\Omega
.

Proof of Theorem
3.1
.

Step one:
We prove
{\rm Min}[\psi_{W}(v)]\neq\emptyset
for all
v>0
. Since
W
is compact,
B^{(v)}(x)\subset\subset\Omega
for
|x|
large. Hence there is
\{E_{j}\}_{j}
with

E_{j}\subset\Omega\,,\,\,\,|E_{j}|=v\,,\,\,\,P(E_{j};\Omega)\leq\min\Big\{P(B^{(v)}),P(F;\Omega)\Big\}+(1/j)\,,

(3.6)

for every
F\subset\Omega
with
|F|=v
. Hence, up to extracting subsequences,
E_{j}\to E
in
L^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})
with
P(E;\Omega)\leq\varliminf_{j\to\infty}P(E_{j};\Omega)
, where
E\subset\Omega
and
|E|\leq v
. We now make three remarks concerning
E
:

(a):

If
\{\Omega_{i}\}_{i\in I}
are the connected components of
\Omega
, then
\Omega\cap\partial^{*}E=\emptyset
if and only
E=\bigcup_{i\in I_{0}}\Omega_{i}
(
I_{0}\subset I
). Indeed,
\Omega\cap\partial^{\ast}E=\emptyset
implies
\mathrm{cl}\,(\partial^{*}E)\cap\Omega=\partial E\cap\Omega
, hence
\partial E\subset\partial\Omega
and
E=\bigcup_{i\in I_{0}}\Omega_{i}
. The converse is immediate.

(b):

If
\Omega\cap\partial^{*}E\neq\emptyset
, then we can construct a system of “volume–fixing variations” for
\{E_{j}\}_{j}
. Indeed, if
\Omega\cap\partial^{*}E\neq\emptyset
, then there are
B_{S_{0}}(x_{0})\subset\subset\Omega
with
P(E;\partial B_{S_{0}}(x_{0}))=0
and a vector field
X\in C^{\infty}_{c}(B_{S_{0}}(x_{0});\mathbb{R}^{n+1})
such that
\int_{E}{\rm div}\,\,X=1
. By
[
Mag12
, Theorem 29.14]
, there are constants
C_{0},c_{0}>0
, depending on
E
itself, with the following property: whenever
|(F\Delta E)\cap B_{S_{0}}(x_{0})|<c_{0}
, then there is a smooth function
\Phi^{F}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\times(-c_{0},c_{0})\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that, for each
|t|<c_{0}
, the map
\Phi_{t}^{F}=\Phi^{F}(\cdot,t)
is a smooth diffeomorphism with
\{\Phi_{t}^{F}\neq{\rm id}\,\}\subset\subset B_{S_{0}}(x_{0})
,
|\Phi_{t}^{F}(F)|=|F|+t
, and
P(\Phi_{t}^{F}(F);B_{S_{0}}(x_{0}))\leq(1+C_{0}\,|t|)\,P(F;B_{S_{0}}(x_{0}))
. For
j
large enough, we evidently have
|(E_{j}\Delta E)\cap B_{S_{0}}(x_{0})|<c_{0}
, and thus we can construct smooth functions
\Phi^{j}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\times(-c_{0},c_{0})\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that, for each
|t|<c_{0}
, the map
\Phi_{t}^{j}=\Phi^{j}(\cdot,t)
is a smooth diffeomorphism with
\{\Phi_{t}^{j}\neq{\rm id}\,\}\subset\subset B_{S_{0}}(x_{0})
,
|\Phi_{t}^{j}(E_{j})|=|E_{j}|+t
, and
P(\Phi_{t}^{j}(E_{j});B_{S_{0}}(x_{0}))\leq(1+C_{0}\,|t|)\,P(E_{j};B_{S_{0}}(x_{0}))
.

(c):

If
\Omega\cap\partial^{*}E\neq\emptyset
, then
E
is bounded
. Since
|E|\leq v<\infty
, it is enough to prove that
\Omega\cap\partial^{*}E
is bounded. In turn, taking
x_{0}\in\Omega\cap\partial^{*}E
, and since
W
is bounded and
|E|<\infty
, the boundedness of
\Omega\cap\partial^{*}E
descends immediately by the following density estimate: there is
r_{1}>0
such that

\begin{split}&|E\cap B_{r}(x)|\geq c(n)\,r^{n+1}\\
&\forall\,\,x\in\Omega\cap\partial^{*}E\,,\,\,r<r_{1}\,,\,\,B_{r}(x)\subset\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus\big(I_{r_{1}}(W)\cup B_{S_{0}}(x_{0})\big)\,.\end{split}

(3.7)

To prove (
3.7
), let
r_{1}>0
be such that
|B_{r_{1}}|<c_{0}
, let
x
and
r
be as in (
3.7
), and set
F_{j}=(\Phi_{t}^{j}(E_{j})\cap B_{S_{0}}(x_{0}))\cup[E_{j}\setminus(B_{r}(x)\cup B_{S_{0}}(x_{0}))]
for
t=|E_{j}\cap B_{r}(x)|
(which is an admissible value of
t
by
|B_{r_{1}}|<c_{0}
). In this way,
|F_{j}|=|E_{j}|=v
, and thus we can exploit (
3.6
) with
F=F_{j}
. A standard argument (see, e.g.
[
Mag12
, Theorem 21.11]
) leads then to (
3.7
).

Now, since
\partial\Omega\subset W
is bounded, every connected component of
\Omega
with finite volume is bounded. Thus, by (a), (b) and (c) above, there is
R>0
such that
W\cup E\,\subset\subset\,B_{R}
. Since
|E\cap[B_{R+1}\setminus B_{R}]|=0
, we can pick
T\in(R,R+1)
such that
\mathcal{H}^{n}(E_{j}\cap\partial B_{T})\to 0
and
P(E_{j}\setminus B_{T})=\mathcal{H}^{n}(E_{j}\cap\partial B_{T})+P(E_{j};\Omega\setminus B_{T})
, and consider the sets
F_{j}=(E_{j}\cap B_{T})\cup B_{\rho_{j}}(y)
corresponding to
\rho_{j}=(|E_{j}\setminus B_{T}|/\omega_{n+1})^{1/(n+1)}
and to
y\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
which is independent from
j
and such that
|y|>\rho_{j}+T
(notice that
\sup_{j}\rho_{j}\leq C(n)\,v^{1/(n+1)}
). Since
|F_{j}|=|E_{j}|=v
, (
3.6
) with
F=F_{j}
and
P(B_{\rho_{j}})\leq P(E_{j}\setminus B_{T})
give

\displaystyle P(E_{j};\Omega)-(1/j)\!\!

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!P(F_{j};\Omega)\leq P(E_{j};\Omega\cap B_{T})+\mathcal{H}^{n}(E_{j}\cap\partial B_{T})+P(B_{\rho_{j}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!P(E_{j};\Omega)+2\,\mathcal{H}^{n}(E_{j}\cap\partial B_{T})\,,

so that, by the choice of
T
,
\{F_{j}\}_{j}
is a minimizing sequence for
\psi_{W}(v)
, with
F_{j}\subset B_{T^{*}}
and
T^{*}
independent of
j
. We conclude by the Direct Method.

Step two:
We prove (
3.2
). If
E_{v}
a minimizer of
\psi_{W}(v)
and
R>0
is such that
W\subset\subset B_{R}
, then by
P(E_{v};\Omega)\leq P(B^{(v)})
we have, for
v>v_{0}
, and
v_{0}
and
C_{0}
depending on
n
and
W
,

\displaystyle P(E_{v}\setminus B_{R})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle P(E_{v};\Omega)+n\,\omega_{n}\,R^{n}\leq P(B^{(v)})+C_{0}

(3.8)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1+(C_{0}/v))\,P(B^{(|E_{v}\setminus B_{R}|)})+C_{0}\,,

where we have used that, if
v>2\,b>0
and
\alpha=n/(n+1)
, then

P(B^{(v)})\,P(B^{(v-b)})^{-1}-1=(v/(v-b))^{\alpha}-1\leq\alpha\,b/(v-b)\leq 2\,\alpha\,b\,v^{-1}\,.

By combining (
1.3
) and (
3.8
) we conclude that, for some
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
,

c(n)\,\Big(\frac{|(E_{v}\setminus B_{R})\Delta B^{(|E_{v}\setminus B_{R}|)}(x)|}{|E_{v}\setminus B_{R}|}\Big)^{2}\leq\frac{P(E_{v}\setminus B_{R})}{P(B^{(|E_{v}\setminus B_{R}|)})}-1\leq\frac{C_{0}}{v^{n/(n+1)}}\,,

provided
v>v_{0}
. Hence we deduce (
3.2
) from

\displaystyle|E_{v}\Delta B^{(v)}(x)|=2\,|E_{v}\setminus B^{(v)}(x)|\leq C_{0}+2\,\big|(E_{v}\setminus B_{R})\setminus B^{(v)}(x)\big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}+2\,\big|(E_{v}\setminus B_{R})\setminus B^{(|E_{v}\setminus B_{R}|)}(x)\big|\leq C_{0}+|E_{v}\setminus B_{R}|\,C_{0}\,v^{-n/2\,(n+1)}\,.

Step three:
We prove the existence of
v_{0}
,
\Lambda_{0}
, and
s_{0}
such that every
E_{v}\in{\rm Min}[\psi_{W}(v)]
with
v>v_{0}
satisfies (
3.1
). Arguing by contradiction, we assume the existence of
v_{j}\to\infty
,
E_{j}\in{\rm Min}[\psi_{W}(v_{j})]
,
F_{j}\subset\Omega
with
|F_{j}\Delta E_{j}|>0
and
F_{j}\Delta E_{j}\subset\subset B_{r_{j}}(x_{j})
for some
x_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
and
r_{j}=v_{j}^{1/(n+1)}/j
, such that

P(E_{j};\Omega\cap B_{r_{j}}(x_{j}))\geq P(F_{j};\Omega\cap B_{r_{j}}(x_{j}))+j\,v_{j}^{-1/(n+1)}\,\big|E_{j}\Delta F_{j}\big|\,.

Denoting by
E_{j}^{*}
,
F_{j}^{*}
and
\Omega_{j}
the sets obtained by scaling
E_{j}
,
F_{j}
and
\Omega
by a factor
v_{j}^{-1/(n+1)}
, we find that
F_{j}^{*}\Delta E_{j}^{*}\subset\subset B_{1/j}(y_{j})
for some
y_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
, and

P(E_{j}^{*};\Omega_{j}\cap B_{1/j}(y_{j}))\geq P(F_{j}^{*};\Omega_{j}\cap B_{1/j}(y_{j}))+j\,\big|E_{j}^{*}\Delta F_{j}^{*}\big|\,.

(3.9)

By (
3.2
) there are
z_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
such that
|E_{j}^{*}\Delta B^{(1)}(z_{j})|\to 0
. We can therefore use the volume-fixing variations of
B^{(1)}
to find diffeomorphisms
\Phi^{j}_{t}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
and constants
c(n)
and
C(n)
such that, for every
|t|<c(n)
, one has
\{\Phi^{j}_{t}\neq{\rm id}\,\}\subset\subset U_{j}
for some open ball
U_{j}
with
U_{j}\subset\subset\Omega_{j}\setminus B_{1/j}(y_{j})
,

|\Phi^{j}_{t}(E_{j}^{*})\cap U_{j}|=|E_{j}^{*}\cap U_{j}|+t
, and
P(\Phi^{j}_{t}(E_{j}^{*});U_{j})\leq(1+C(n)\,|t|)\,P(E_{j}^{*};U_{j})
. Since
F_{j}^{*}\Delta E_{j}^{*}\subset\subset B_{1/j}(y_{j})
implies
||F_{j}^{*}|-|E_{j}^{*}||<c(n)
for
j
large, if
t=|E_{j}^{*}|-|F_{j}^{*}|
, then
G_{j}^{*}=\Phi^{j}_{t}(F_{j}^{*})
is such that
|G_{j}^{*}|=|E_{j}^{*}|
, and by
E_{j}\in{\rm Min}[\psi_{W}(v_{j})]
,

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!P(E_{j}^{*};\Omega_{j})\leq P(G_{j}^{*};\Omega_{j})\leq P\big(E_{j}^{*};\Omega_{j}\setminus(U_{j}\cup B_{1/j}(y_{j}))\big)

\displaystyle+P(F_{j}^{*};\Omega_{j}\cap B_{1/j}(y_{j}))+P(E_{j}^{*};U_{j})+C(n)\,P(E_{j}^{*};U_{j})\,\big|E_{j}^{*}\Delta F_{j}^{*}\big|\,.

Taking into account
P(E^{*}_{j};U_{j})\leq\psi_{W}(v_{j})/v_{j}^{n/(n+1)}\leq C(n)
, we thus find

\displaystyle P(E_{j}^{*};\Omega_{j}\cap B_{1/j}(y_{j}))\leq P(F_{j}^{*};\Omega_{j}\cap B_{1/j}(y_{j}))+C(n)\,\big|E_{j}^{*}\Delta F_{j}^{*}\big|\,,

which, by (
3.9
), gives
j\,\big|E_{j}^{*}\Delta F_{j}^{*}\big|\leq C(n)\,\big|E_{j}^{*}\Delta F_{j}^{*}\big|
. Since
|E_{j}^{*}\Delta F_{j}^{*}|>0
, this is a contradiction for
j
large enough.

Step four:
We now prove that, if
\mathcal{R}(W)>0
, then

\lim_{v\to\infty}\,\sup_{E_{v}\in{\rm Min}[\psi_{W}(v)]}\,\big||x|\,v^{-1/(n+1)}-\omega_{n+1}^{-1/(n+1)}\big|=0\,,

(3.10)

where
x
is related to
E_{v}
by (
3.2
). In proving (
3.10
) we will use the assumption
\mathcal{R}(W)>0
and the energy upper bound

\varlimsup_{v\to\infty}\psi_{W}(v)-P(B^{(v)})\leq-\mathcal{R}(W)\,.

(3.11)

A proof of (
3.11
) is given in step one of the proof of Theorem
1.6
, see section
5
; in turn, that proof is solely based on the results from section
4
, where no part of Theorem
3.1
(not even the existence of minimizers in
\psi_{W}(v)
) is ever used. This said, when
|W|>0
, and thus
\mathcal{S}(W)>0
, one can replace (
3.11
) in the proof of (
3.10
) by the simpler upper bound

\varlimsup_{v\to\infty}\psi_{W}(v)-P(B^{(v)})\leq-\mathcal{S}(W)\,,

(3.12)

where, we recall,
\mathcal{S}(W)=\sup\{\mathcal{H}^{n}(W\cap\Pi):\mbox{$\Pi$ is a hyperplane in $\mathbb{R}^{n+1}$}\}
. To prove (
3.12
), given
\Pi
, we construct competitors for
\psi_{W}(v)
by intersecting
\Omega
with balls
B^{(v^{\prime})}(x_{v})
with
v^{\prime}>v
and
x_{v}
such that

|B^{(v^{\prime})}(x_{v})\setminus W|=v
and
\mathcal{H}^{n}(W\cap\partial B^{(v^{\prime})}(x_{v}))\to\mathcal{H}^{n}(W\cap\Pi)
as
v\to\infty
. Hence,

\varlimsup_{v\to\infty}\psi_{W}(v)-P(B^{(v)})\leq-\mathcal{H}^{n}(W\cap\Pi)
,
thus giving (
3.12
). The proof of (
3.11
) is identical in spirit to that of (
3.12
), with the difference that to glue a large ball to
(F,\nu)\in{\rm Max}[\mathcal{R}(W)]
we will need to establish the decay of
\partial F
towards a hyperplane parallel to
\nu^{\perp}
to the high degree of precision expressed in (
1.14
). Now to prove (
3.10
): by contradiction, consider
v_{j}\to\infty
,
E_{j}\in{\rm Min}[\psi_{W}(v_{j})]
, and
x_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
with
\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}}|E_{j}\Delta B^{(v_{j})}(x)|=|E_{j}\Delta B^{(v_{j})}(x_{j})|
, such that

\varliminf_{j\to\infty}\big||x_{j}|\,v_{j}^{-1/(n+1)}-\omega_{n+1}^{-1/(n+1)}\big|>0\,,

(3.13)

and set
\lambda_{j}=v_{j}^{-1/(n+1)}
,
E_{j}^{*}=\lambda_{j}\,(E_{j}-x_{j})
,
W_{j}^{*}=\lambda_{j}\,(W-x_{j})
, and
\Omega_{j}^{*}=\lambda_{j}\,(\Omega-x_{j})
. By (
3.1
), each
E_{j}^{*}
is a
(\Lambda_{0},s_{0})
-perimeter minimizer with free boundary in
\Omega_{j}^{*}
. By (
3.2
) and the defining property of
x_{j}
,
E_{j}^{*}\to B^{(1)}
in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})
. Moreover,
{\rm diam}\,(W_{j}^{*})\to 0
and, by (
3.13
),

\varliminf_{j\to\infty}{\rm dist}\big(W_{j}^{*},\partial B^{(1)}\big)>0\,.

(3.14)

Thus there is
z_{0}\not\in\partial B^{(1)}
such that, for every
\rho<{\rm dist}(z_{0},\partial B^{(1)})
, there is
j(\rho)
such that
\{E_{j}^{*}\}_{j\geq j(\rho)}
is a sequence of
(\Lambda_{0},s_{0})
-perimeter minimizers in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus B_{\rho/2}(z_{0})
. By Remark
3.2
, up to increasing
j(\rho)
,
(\partial E_{j}^{*})\setminus B_{\rho}(z_{0})
is contained in the normal graph over
\partial B^{(1)}
of
u_{j}
with
\|u_{j}\|_{C^{1}(\partial B^{(1)})}\to 0
; in particular, by (
3.14
),
(\partial E_{j}^{*})\setminus B_{\rho}(z_{0})
is disjoint from
W_{j}^{*}
. By the constant mean curvature condition satisfied by
\Omega\cap\partial E_{j}^{*}
, and by Alexandrov’s theorem
[
Ale62
]
,
(\partial E_{j}^{*})\setminus B_{\rho}(z_{0})
is a sphere
M_{j}^{*}
for
j\geq j(\rho)
. Let
B_{j}^{*}
be the ball bounded by
M_{j}^{*}
. Since
M_{j}^{*}\cap W_{j}^{*}=\emptyset
, we have either one of the following:

Case one:

W_{j}^{*}\subset B_{j}^{*}
. We have
\partial[B_{j}^{*}\cup E_{j}^{*}]\subset M_{j}^{*}\cup[(\partial E_{j}^{*})\setminus\mathrm{cl}\,(B_{j}^{*})]\subset(\partial E_{j}^{*})\setminus W_{j}^{*}
, so that, by
|B_{j}^{*}\cup E_{j}^{*}|\geq|E_{j}^{*}|+|W_{j}^{*}|\geq 1
, we find
P(E_{j}^{*};\Omega_{j}^{*})\geq P(B_{j}^{*}\cup E_{j}^{*})\geq P(B^{(1)})
, that is,
\psi_{W}(v_{j})\geq P(B^{(1)})
, against (
3.11
).

Case two:

W_{j}^{*}\cap B_{j}^{*}=\emptyset
. In this case,
E_{j}^{*}=B_{j}^{*}\cup G_{j}^{*}
, where
G_{j}^{*}
is the union of the connected components of
E_{j}^{*}
whose boundaries have non-empty intersection with
W_{j}^{*}
: in other words, we are claiming that
B_{j}^{*}
is the only connected component of
E_{j}^{*}
whose closure is disjoint from
W_{j}^{*}
. Indeed, if this were not the case, we could recombine all the connected components of
E_{j}^{*}
with closure disjoint from
W_{j}^{*}
into a single ball of same total volume, centered far away from
W_{j}^{*}
, in such a way to strictly decrease
P(E_{j}^{*};\Omega_{j}^{*})
, against
E_{j}\in{\rm Min}[\psi_{W}(v_{j})]
. Let us now set

G_{j}=x_{j}+v_{j}^{1/(n+1)}\,G_{j}^{*}
and
U_{j}=x_{j}+v_{j}^{1/(n+1)}\,B_{j}^{*}
, so that
E_{j}=G_{j}\cup U_{j}
and
{\rm dist}(G_{j},U_{j})>0
.

If we start sliding
U_{j}
from infinity towards
G_{j}\cup W
along arbitrary directions, then at least one of the resulting “contact points”
z_{j}
belongs to
\Omega\cap\partial G_{j}
: if this were not the case, then
G_{j}
would be contained in the convex envelope of
W
, so that
|B_{j}|=|E_{j}|-|G_{j}|\geq v_{j}-C(W)
, and thus, by
\psi_{W}(v_{j})=P(E_{j};\Omega)\geq P(B_{j};W)=P(B_{j})
, and by
P(B_{j})\geq P(B^{(v_{j}-C(W))})\geq P(B^{(v_{j})})-C(W)\,v_{j}^{-1/(n+1)}
, against with (
3.11
) for
j
large.

By construction, there is a half-space
H_{j}
such that
G_{j}\subset H_{j}
,
z_{j}\in(\partial G_{j})\cap(\partial H_{j})
, and
G_{j}
is a perimeter minimizer in
B_{r}(z_{j})
for some small
r>0
. By the strong maximum principle, see, e.g.
[
DPM15
, Lemma 2.13]
,
G_{j}
has
H_{j}-z_{j}
as its unique blowup at
z_{j}
. By De Giorgi’s regularity theorem, see e.g.
[
Mag12
, Part III]
,
G_{j}
is an open set with smooth boundary in a neighborhood of
z_{j}
. Therefore, if we denote by
U_{j}^{\prime}
the translation of
U_{j}
constructed in the sliding argument, then,
E_{j}^{\prime}=G_{j}\cup U_{j}^{\prime}\in{\rm Min}[\psi_{W}(v)]
and, in a neighborhood of
z_{j}
,
E_{j}^{\prime}
is the union of two disjoint sets with smooth boundary which touch tangentially at
z_{j}
. In particular,
|E_{j}^{\prime}\cap B_{r}(z_{j})|/|B_{r}|\to 1
as
r\to 0^{+}
, against volume density estimates implied by (
3.1
), see, e.g.
[
Mag12
, Theorem 21.11]
.

Step five:
We finally show the existence of
v_{0}
and
R_{0}(v)
with
R_{0}(v)\to 0^{+}
and
R_{0}(v)\,v^{1/(n+1)}\to\infty
, such that each
E_{v}\in{\rm Min}[\psi_{W}(v)]
with
v>v_{0}
determines
x
and
u\in C^{\infty}(\partial B^{(1)})
such that (
3.3
) holds and
\sup_{E_{v}}\|u\|_{C^{1}(\partial B^{(1)})}\to 0
as
v\to\infty
. To this end, let us consider
v_{j}\to\infty
,
E_{j}\in{\rm Min}[\psi_{W}(v_{j})]
, and define
x_{j}
,
E_{j}^{*}
and
W_{j}^{*}
as in step four. Thanks to (
3.10
), there is
z_{0}\in\partial B^{(1)}
s.t.
{\rm dist}(z_{0},W_{j}^{*})\to 0
. In particular, for every
\rho>0
, we can find
j(\rho)\in\mathbb{N}
such that if
j\geq j(\rho)
, then
E_{j}^{*}
is a
(\Lambda_{0},s_{0})
-perimeter minimizer in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus B_{\rho}(z_{0})
, with
E_{j}^{*}\to B^{(1)}
. By Remark
3.2
, there are
u_{j}\in C^{1}(\partial B^{(1)})
such that

(\partial E_{j}^{*})\setminus B_{2\,\rho}(z_{0})=\big\{y+u_{j}(y)\,\nu_{B^{(1)}}(y):y\in\partial B^{(1)}\big\}\setminus B_{2\,\rho}(z_{0})\,,\,\,\forall j\geq j(\rho)\,,

and
\|u_{j}\|_{C^{1}(\partial B^{(1)})}\to 0
. By the arbitrariness of
\rho
and by a contradiction argument, (
3.3
) holds with
R_{0}(v)\to 0^{+}
such that
R_{0}(v)\,v^{1/(n+1)}\to\infty
as
v\to\infty
, and with the uniform decay of
\|u\|_{C^{1}(\partial B^{(1)})}
.
∎

4.
Properties of isoperimetric residues

Here we prove Theorem
1.1
. It will be convenient to introduce some notation for cylinders and slabs in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
: precisely, given
r>0
,
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{n}
and
I\subset\mathbb{R}
, and setting
\mathbf{p}_{\nu^{\perp}}(x)=x-(x\cdot\nu)\,\nu
(
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
), we let

\displaystyle\textbf{D}_{r}^{\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}:|\mathbf{p}_{\nu^{\perp}}x|<r\,,x\cdot\nu=0\big\}\,,

\displaystyle\textbf{C}_{r}^{\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}:|\mathbf{p}_{\nu^{\perp}}x|<r\big\}\,,

\displaystyle\textbf{C}_{r,I}^{\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}:|\mathbf{p}_{\nu^{\perp}}x|<r\,,x\cdot\nu\in I\big\}\,,

(4.1)

\displaystyle\partial_{\ell}\textbf{C}_{r,I}^{\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}:|\mathbf{p}_{\nu^{\perp}}x|=r\,,x\cdot\nu\in I\big\}\,,

\displaystyle\textbf{S}_{I}^{\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}:x\cdot\nu\in I\big\}\,.

In each case, given
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
, we also set
\textbf{D}_{r}^{\nu}(x)=x+\textbf{D}_{r}^{\nu}
,
\textbf{C}_{r}^{\nu}(x)=x+\textbf{C}_{r}^{\nu}
, etc. We premise the following proposition, used in the proof of Theorem
1.1
and Theorem
1.6
, and based on
[
Sch83
, Proposition 1 and Proposition 3]
.

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
n\geq 2
,
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{n}
, and let
f
be a Lipschitz solution to the minimal surface equation on
\nu^{\perp}\setminus\mathrm{cl}\,(\textbf{{D}}_{R}^{\nu})
.
If
n=2
, assume in addition that
M=\{x+f(x)\,\nu:|x|>R\}
is stable and has natural area growth, i.e.

\displaystyle\int_{M}\,|\nabla^{M}\varphi|^{2}-|A|^{2}\,\varphi^{2}\geq 0\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\forall\varphi\in C^{1}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus B_{R})\,,

(4.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{2}(M\cap B_{r})\leq C\,r^{2}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\forall r>R\,.

(4.3)

Then there are
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
and
c\in\nu^{\perp}
such that, for every
|x|>R
,

\displaystyle\big|f(x)-\big(a+b\,|x|^{2-n}+(c\cdot x)\,|x|^{-n}\big)\big|\leq C\,|x|^{-n}\,,\,\,(n\geq 3)

(4.4)

\displaystyle\big|f(x)-\big(a+b\,\log|x|+(c\cdot x)\,|x|^{-2}\big)\big|\leq C\,|x|^{-2}\,,\,\,(n=2)

(4.5)

\displaystyle\max\Big\{|x|^{n-1}\,|\nabla f(x)|,|x|^{n}\,|\nabla^{2}f(x)|:|x|>R\Big\}\leq C\,,\,\,(\mbox{every $n$})\,.

(4.6)

Proof.

If
n\geq 3
, the fact that
\nabla f
is bounded allows one to represent
f
as the convolution with a singular kernel which, by a classical result of Littman, Stampacchia, and Weinberger
[
LSW63
]
, is comparable to the Green’s function of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
; (
4.4
) is then deduced starting from that representation formula. For more details, see
[
Sch83
, Proposition 3]
. In the case
n=2
, by (
4.2
) and (
4.3
), we can exploit a classical “logarithmic cut-off argument” to see that
M
has finite total curvature, i.e.
\int_{M}|K|\,d\mathcal{H}^{2}<\infty
, where
K
is the Gaussian curvature of
M
. As a consequence, see, e.g.
[
PR02
, Section 1.2]
, the compactification
\overline{M}
of
M
is a Riemann surface with boundary, and
M
is conformally equivalent to
\overline{M}\setminus\{p_{1},...,p_{m}\}
, where
p_{i}
are interior points of
\overline{M}
. One can thus conclude by the argument in
[
Sch83
, Proposition 1]
that
M
has
m
-many ends satisfying the decay (
4.5
), and then that
m=1
thanks to the fact that
M=\{x+f(x)\,\nu:|x|>R\}
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

Step one:
Given a hyperplane
\Pi
in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
, if
F
is a half-space with
\partial F=\Pi
and
\nu
is a unit normal to
\Pi
, then
{\rm res}_{W}(F,\nu)=\mathcal{H}^{n}(W\cap\Pi)
. Therefore the lower bound in (
1.11
) follows by

\mathcal{R}(W)\geq\mathcal{S}(W)=\sup\big\{\mathcal{H}^{n}(\Pi\cap W):\mbox{$\Pi$ an hyperplane in $\mathbb{R}^{n+1}$}\big\}\,.

(4.7)

Step two:
We notice that, if
(F,\nu)\in\mathcal{F}
, then by (
1.8
), (
1.9
), and the divergence theorem (see, e.g.,
[
Mag12
, Lemma 22.11]
), we can define a Radon measure on the open set
\nu^{\perp}\setminus\mathbf{p}_{\nu^{\perp}}(W)
by setting

\mu(U)=P\big(F;(\mathbf{p}_{\nu^{\perp}})^{-1}(U)\big)-\mathcal{H}^{n}(U)\,,\qquad U\subset\nu^{\perp}\setminus\mathbf{p}_{\nu^{\perp}}(W)\,.

(4.8)

In particular, setting
R^{\prime}=\inf\{\rho:W\subset\textbf{{C}}_{\rho}^{\nu}\}
,
\mu(\textbf{{D}}_{R}^{\nu}\setminus\mathbf{p}_{\nu^{\perp}}(W))\geq 0
gives

P(F;\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu}\setminus W)\geq\omega_{n}\,R^{n}-\mathcal{H}^{n}(\mathbf{p}_{\nu^{\perp}}(W))\,,\qquad\forall R>R^{\prime}\,,

while the identity

\displaystyle\omega_{n}\,R^{n}-P(F;\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu}\setminus W)=-\mu(\textbf{{D}}_{R}^{\nu}\setminus\textbf{{D}}_{R^{\prime}}^{\nu})+\omega_{n}\,(R^{\prime})^{n}-P(F;\textbf{{C}}_{R^{\prime}}^{\nu}\setminus W)

(which possibly holds as
-\infty=-\infty
if
P(F;\textbf{{C}}_{R^{\prime}}^{\nu}\setminus W)=+\infty
) gives that

R\in(R^{\prime},\infty)\mapsto\omega_{n}\,R^{n}-P(F;\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu}\setminus W)\,\,\,\mbox{is {\it decreasing} on $(R^{\prime},\infty)$}\,.

(4.9)

In particular, the limsup defining
{\rm res}_{W}
always exists as a limit.

Step three:
We prove the existence of
(F,\nu)\in{\rm Max}[\mathcal{R}(W)]
and (
1.12
). We first claim that if
\{(F_{j},\nu_{j})\}_{j}
is a maximizing sequence for
\mathcal{R}(W)
, then, in addition to
\mathbf{p}_{\nu_{j}^{\perp}}(\partial F_{j})=\nu_{j}^{\perp}
, one can modify
(F_{j},\nu_{j})
, preserving the optimality in the limit
j\to\infty
, so that (setting
X\subset^{\mathcal{L}^{n+1}}Y
for
|X\setminus Y|=0
)

\displaystyle\partial F_{j}\subset\textbf{S}_{[A_{j},B_{j}]}^{\nu_{j}}\,,\,\,\,\textbf{S}_{(-\infty,A_{j})}^{\nu_{j}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{L}^{n+1}}}{{\subset}}F_{j}\,,\,\,\,\textbf{S}_{(B_{j},\infty)}^{\nu_{j}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{L}^{n+1}}}{{\subset}}\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus F_{j}\,,\hskip 14.22636pt

(4.10)

\displaystyle\mbox{where}\,\,[A_{j},B_{j}]=\bigcap\big\{(\alpha,\beta):W\subset\textbf{S}_{(\alpha,\beta)}^{\nu_{j}}\big\}\,.

(4.11)

Indeed, since
(F_{j},\nu_{j})\in\mathcal{F}
, for some
\alpha_{j}<\beta_{j}\in\mathbb{R}
we have

\partial F_{j}\subset\textbf{S}_{[\alpha_{j},\beta_{j}]}^{\nu_{j}}\,,\qquad\mathbf{p}_{\nu_{j}^{\perp}}(\partial F_{j})=\nu_{j}^{\perp}\,.

(4.12)

Would it be that either
\textbf{S}_{(-\infty,\alpha_{j})\cup(\beta_{j},\infty)}^{\nu_{j}}\subset_{\mathcal{L}^{n+1}}F_{j}
or
\textbf{S}_{(-\infty,\alpha_{j})\cup(\beta_{j},\infty)}^{\nu_{j}}\subset_{\mathcal{L}^{n+1}}\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus F_{j}
, then, by the divergence theorem and by
\mathbf{p}_{\nu_{j}^{\perp}}(\partial F_{j})=\nu_{j}^{\perp}
,

P(F_{j};\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu_{j}}\cap\Omega)\geq 2\,\big(\omega_{n}\,R^{n}-\mathcal{H}^{n}(\mathbf{p}_{\nu_{j}^{\perp}}(W))\big)\,,\qquad\forall R>0\,,

and thus
{\rm res}_{W}(F_{j},\nu_{j})=-\infty
; in particular,
(F_{j},\nu_{j})\in\mathcal{F}
being a maximizing sequence, we would have
\mathcal{R}(W)=-\infty
, against (
4.7
). This proves the validity (up to switching
F_{j}
with
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus F_{j}
), of the inclusions

\textbf{S}_{(-\infty,\alpha_{j})}^{\nu_{j}}\subset_{\mathcal{L}^{n+1}}F_{j}\,,\qquad\textbf{S}_{(\beta_{j},\infty)}^{\nu_{j}}\subset_{\mathcal{L}^{n+1}}\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus F_{j}\,.

(4.13)

Thanks to (
4.13
) (and by exploiting basic set operations on sets of finite perimeter, see, e.g.,
[
Mag12
, Theorem 16.3]
), we see that

F_{j}^{*}=\big(F_{j}\cup\textbf{S}_{(-\infty,A_{j}-1/j)}^{\nu_{j}}\big)\cap\textbf{S}_{(-\infty,B_{j}+1/j)}^{\nu_{j}}
satisfies

(4.14)

\displaystyle(F_{j}^{*},\nu_{j})\in\mathcal{F}\,,\qquad P\big(F_{j}^{*};\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu_{j}}\setminus W\big)\leq P\big(F_{j};\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu_{j}}\setminus W\big)\,,\qquad\forall R>0\,;

in particular,
\{(F_{j}^{*},\nu_{j})\}_{j}
is also a maximizing sequence for
\mathcal{R}(W)
. By standard compactness theorems there are
F
of locally finite perimeter in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
and
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{n}
such that
F_{j}\to F
in
L^{1}_{{\rm loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})
and
\nu_{j}\to\nu
. If
A\subset\subset\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu}\setminus W
is open, then, for
j
large enough,
A\subset\subset\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu_{j}}\setminus W
, and thus

P(F;\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu}\setminus W)=\sup_{A\subset\subset\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu}\setminus W}\,P(F;A)\leq\varliminf_{j\to\infty}P(F_{j};\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu_{j}}\setminus W)\,.

(4.15)

By (
4.9
),
R\mapsto\omega_{n}\,R^{n}-P(F_{j};\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu_{j}}\setminus W)
is decreasing on
R>R_{j}=\inf\{\rho:W\subset\textbf{{C}}_{\rho}^{\nu_{j}}\}
. By
\sup_{j}R_{j}\leq C(W)<\infty
and (
4.15
) we have

\omega_{n}\,R^{n}-P(F;\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu}\setminus W)\geq\varlimsup_{j\to\infty}\omega_{n}\,R^{n}-P(F_{j};\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu_{j}}\setminus W)\geq\varlimsup_{j\to\infty}{\rm res}_{W}(F_{j},\nu_{j})\,,

for every
R>C(W)
; in particular, letting
R\to\infty
,

{\rm res}_{W}(F,\nu)\geq\varlimsup_{j\to\infty}{\rm res}_{W}(F_{j},\nu_{j})=\mathcal{R}(W)\,.

(4.16)

By
F_{j}\to F
in
L^{1}_{{\rm loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})
,
\partial F=\mathrm{cl}\,(\partial^{*}F)
is contained in the set of accumulation points of sequences
\{x_{j}\}_{j}
with
x_{j}\in\partial F_{j}
, so that (
4.10
) gives

\partial F\subset\textbf{S}_{[A,B]}^{\nu}\,,\qquad\textbf{S}_{(-\infty,A)}^{\nu}\subset_{\mathcal{L}^{n+1}}F\,,\qquad\textbf{S}_{(B,\infty)}^{\nu}\subset_{\mathcal{L}^{n+1}}\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus F\,,

(4.17)

if
[A,B]=\bigcap\{(\alpha,\beta):W\subset\textbf{S}^{\nu}_{(\alpha,\beta)}\}
. Therefore
(F,\nu)\in\mathcal{F}
, and thus, by (
4.16
),
(F,\nu)\in{\rm Max}[\mathcal{R}(W)]
. We now show that (
4.16
) implies (
1.12
), i.e.

P(F;\Omega\cap B)\leq P(G;\Omega\cap B)\,,\qquad\mbox{$\forall F\Delta G\subset\subset B$, $B$ a ball}\,.

(4.18)

Indeed, should (
4.18
) fail, we could find
\delta>0
and
G\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
with
F\Delta G\subset\subset B
for some ball
B
, such that
P(G;B\setminus W)+\delta\leq P(F;B\setminus W)
. For
R
large enough to entail
B\subset\subset\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu}
we would then find

{\rm res}_{W}(F,\nu)+\delta\leq\omega_{n}\,R^{n}-P(F;\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu}\setminus W)+\delta\leq\omega_{n}\,R^{n}-P(G;\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu}\setminus W)\,,

which, letting
R\to\infty
, would violate the maximality of
(F,\nu)
in
\mathcal{R}(W)
.

Step four:
We show that if
\mathcal{R}(W)>0
and
(F,\nu)\in{\rm Max}[\mathcal{R}(W)]
, then
\partial F\subset\mathbf{S}_{[A,B]}^{\nu}
for
A,B
as in (
4.17
). Otherwise, by the same truncation procedure leading to (
4.14
) and by
(F,\nu)\in{\rm Max}[\mathcal{R}(W)]
, we would find

\omega_{n}R^{n}-P\big(F^{*};\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu_{j}}\setminus W\big)\geq\omega_{n}R^{n}-P\big(F;\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu_{j}}\setminus W\big)\geq\mathcal{R}(W)\qquad\forall R>0\,,

so that
(F^{\ast},\nu)\in{\rm Max}[\mathcal{R}(W)]
too. Now
P\big(F;\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu_{j}}\setminus W\big)-P\big(F^{*};\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu_{j}}\setminus W\big)
is increasing in
R
, and since
{\rm res}_{W}(F,\nu)={\rm res}_{W}(F^{\ast},\nu)
, it follows that
P\big(F;\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu_{j}}\setminus W\big)=P\big(F^{*};\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu_{j}}\setminus W\big)
for large
R
. But this can hold only if
\partial F\cap\Omega
is an hyperplane disjoint from
W
, in which case
\mathcal{R}(W)={\rm res}_{W}(F,\nu)=0
.

Step five:
Still assuming
\mathcal{R}(W)>0
, we complete the proof of statement (ii) by proving (
1.14
). By (
4.17
), if
(F,\nu)\in{\rm Max}[\mathcal{R}(W)]
, then
F/R\to H^{-}=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}:x\cdot\nu<0\}
in
L^{1}_{{\rm loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})
as
R\to\infty
. By (
4.18
) and by improved convergence (i.e., Remark
3.2
– notice carefully that
\partial F
is bounded in the direction
\nu
thanks to step four), we find
R_{F}>0
and functions
\{f_{R}\}_{R>R_{F}}\subset C^{1}(\textbf{{D}}_{2}^{\nu}\setminus\textbf{{D}}_{1}^{\nu})
such that

\big(\textbf{{C}}_{2}^{\nu}\setminus\textbf{{C}}_{1}^{\nu}\big)\cap\partial(F/R)=\big\{x+f_{R}(x)\,\nu:x\in\textbf{{D}}_{2}^{\nu}\setminus\textbf{{D}}_{1}^{\nu}\big\}\,,\qquad\forall R>R_{F}\,.

with
\|f_{R}\|_{C^{1}(\textbf{{D}}_{2}^{\nu}\setminus\textbf{{D}}_{1}^{\nu})}\to 0
as
R\to\infty
. Scaling back to
F
we deduce that

(\partial F)\setminus\textbf{{C}}_{R_{F}}^{\nu}=\big\{x+f(x)\,\nu:x\in\nu^{\perp}\setminus\textbf{{D}}_{R_{F}}^{\nu}\big\}\,,

(4.19)

for a (necessarily smooth) solution
f
to the minimal surfaces equation with

\|f\|_{C^{0}(\nu^{\perp}\setminus\textbf{{D}}_{R_{F}}^{\nu})}\leq B-A\,,\qquad\lim_{R\to\infty}\|\nabla f\|_{C^{0}(\textbf{{D}}_{2\,R}^{\nu}\setminus\textbf{{D}}_{R}^{\nu})}=0\,,

(4.20)

thanks to the fact that
f(x)=R\,f_{R}(x/R)
if
x\in\textbf{{D}}_{2\,R}^{\nu}\setminus\textbf{{D}}_{R}^{\nu}
.
When
n\geq 3
, (
1.14
) follows by (
4.19
) and Proposition
4.1
.
When
n=2
, (
4.2
) holds by (
4.18
). To check (
4.3
), we deduce by
{\rm res}_{W}(F,\nu)\geq 0
the existence of
R^{\prime}>R_{F}
such that
\omega_{n}\,R^{n}\geq P(F;\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu}\setminus W)-1
if
R>R^{\prime}
. In particular, setting
M=(\partial F)\setminus B_{R_{F}}
, for
R>R^{\prime}
we have

\mathcal{H}^{2}(M\cap B_{R})\leq\mathcal{H}^{2}(M\cap W)+P(F;\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu}\setminus W)\leq\omega_{n}\,R^{n}+1+\mathcal{H}^{2}(M\cap W)\leq C\,R^{n}\,,

provided
C=\omega_{n}+[(1+\mathcal{H}^{2}(M\cap W))/(R^{\prime})^{n}]
; while if
R\in(R_{F},R^{\prime})
, then
\mathcal{H}^{2}(M\cap B_{R})\leq C\,R^{n}
with
C=\mathcal{H}^{2}(M\cap B_{R^{\prime}})/R_{F}^{n}
. This said, we can apply Proposition
4.1
to deduce (
4.5
). Since
\partial F
is bounded in a slab, the logarithmic term in (
4.5
) must vanish (i.e. (
4.5
) holds with
b=0
), and thus (
1.14
) is proved.
Finally, when
n=1
, by (
4.19
) and (
4.20
) there are
a_{1},a_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
,
x_{1}<x_{2}
,
x_{1},x_{2}\in\nu^{\perp}\equiv\mathbb{R}
such that
f(x)=a_{1}
for
x\in\nu^{\perp}
,
x<x_{1}
, and
f(x)=a_{2}
for
x\in\nu^{\perp}
,
x>x_{2}
. Now, setting
M_{1}=\{x+a_{1}\,\nu:x\in\nu^{\perp},x<x_{1}\}
and
M_{2}=\{x+a_{2}\,\nu:x\in\nu^{\perp},x>x_{2}\}
, we have that

P(F;\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu}\setminus W)=\mathcal{H}^{n}\big(\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu}\cap(\partial F)\setminus(W\cup M_{1}\cup M_{2})\big)+2\,R-|x_{2}-x_{1}|\,;

while, if
L
denotes the line through
x_{1}+a_{1}\,\nu
and
x_{2}+a_{2}\,\nu
, then we can find
\nu_{L}\in\mathbb{S}^{1}
and a set
F_{L}
such that
(F_{L},\nu_{L})\in\mathcal{F}
with
\partial F_{L}=\big[\big((\partial F)\setminus(M_{1}\cup M_{2})\big)\cup(L_{1}\cup L_{2})\big]
, where
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
are the two half-lines obtained by removing from
L
the segment joining
x_{1}+a_{1}\,\nu
and
x_{2}+a_{2}\,\nu
. In this way,
P(F_{L};\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu_{L}}\setminus W)=\mathcal{H}^{n}\big(\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu}\cap(\partial F)\setminus(W\cup M_{1}\cup M_{2})\big)+2\,R-\big|(x_{1}+a_{1}\,\nu)-(x_{2}+a_{2}\,\nu)\big|
, so that
{\rm res}_{W}(F_{L},\nu_{L})-{\rm res}_{W}(F,\nu)=\big|(x_{1}+a_{1}\,\nu)-(x_{2}+a_{2}\,\nu)\big|-|x_{2}-x_{1}|>0
, against
(F,\nu)\in{\rm Max}[\mathcal{R}(W)]
if
a_{1}\neq a_{2}
. Hence,
a_{1}=a_{2}
.

We are left to prove that (
4.19
) holds with
R_{2}=R_{2}(W)
in place of
R_{F}
, and the constants
a
,
b
,
c
and
C_{0}
appearing in (
1.14
) can be bounded in terms of
W
only. To this end, we notice that the argument presented in step one shows that
{\rm Max}[\mathcal{R}(W)]
is pre-compact in
L^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})
. Using this fact and a contradiction argument based on improved convergence (Remark
3.2
), we conclude the proof of statement (ii).

Step six:
We complete the proof of statement (i) and begin the proof of statement (iii) by showing that, setting for brevity
d={\rm diam}\,(W)
, it holds

\mathcal{H}^{n}(W\cap\Pi)\leq\mathcal{R}(W)\leq\sup_{\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{n}}\mathcal{H}^{n}(\mathbf{p}_{\nu^{\perp}}(W))\leq\omega_{n}\,(d/2)^{n}\,,

(4.21)

whenever
\Pi
is a hyperplane in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
. We have already proved the first inequality in step one. To prove the others, we notice that, if
(F,\nu)\in\mathcal{F}
, then
\mathbf{p}_{\nu^{\perp}}(\partial F)=\nu^{\perp}
and (
4.9
)
give, for every
R>R^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!-{\rm res}_{W}(F,\nu)\geq P(F;\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu}\setminus W)-\omega_{n}\,R^{n}\geq\mathcal{H}^{n}\big(\mathbf{p}_{\nu^{\perp}}(\partial F\setminus W)\cap\textbf{{D}}_{R}^{\nu}\big)-\omega_{n}\,R^{n}

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!=-\mathcal{H}^{n}\big(\textbf{{D}}_{R}^{\nu}\setminus\mathbf{p}_{\nu^{\perp}}(\partial F\setminus W)\big)\geq-\mathcal{H}^{n}(\mathbf{p}_{\nu^{\perp}}(W))\geq-\omega_{n}\,(d/2)^{n}\,,

(4.22)

where in the last step we have used the isodiametric inequality. Maximizing over
(F,\nu)
in (
4.22
) we complete the proof of (
4.21
). Moreover, if
W=\mathrm{cl}\,(B_{d/2})
, then, since
\mathcal{S}(\mathrm{cl}\,(B_{d/2}))=\mathcal{H}^{n}(\mathrm{cl}\,(B_{d/2})\cap\Pi)=\omega_{n}\,(d/2)^{n}
for any hyperplane
\Pi
through the origin, we find that
\mathcal{R}(\mathrm{cl}\,(B_{d/2}))=\omega_{n}\,(d/2)^{n}
; in particular, (
4.21
) implies (
1.15
).

Step seven:
We continue the proof of statement (iii) by showing (
1.16
). Let
\mathcal{R}(W)=\omega_{n}\,(d/2)^{n}
and let
(F,\nu)\in{\rm Max}[\mathcal{R}(W)]
. Since every inequality in (
4.22
) holds as an equality, we find in particular that

\displaystyle\sup_{R>R^{\prime}}P(F;\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu}\setminus W)-\mathcal{H}^{n}\big(\mathbf{p}_{\nu^{\perp}}(\partial F\setminus W)\cap\textbf{{D}}_{R}^{\nu}\big)=0\,,

(4.23)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{n}(\mathbf{p}_{\nu^{\perp}}(W))=\omega_{n}\,(d/2)^{n}\,.

(4.24)

By (
4.24
) and the discussion of the equality cases for the isodiametric inequality (see, e.g.
[
MPP14
]
), we see that, for some
x_{0}\in\nu^{\perp}
,

\mathbf{p}_{\nu^{\perp}}(W)=\mathrm{cl}\,(\textbf{{D}}_{d/2}^{\nu}(x_{0}))\,,\qquad\mbox{so that $W\subset\textbf{{C}}_{d/2}^{\nu}(x_{0})$}\,.

(4.25)

Condition (
4.23
) implies that (
1.14
) holds with
u\equiv a
for some
a\in[A,B]=\bigcap\{(\alpha,\beta):W\subset\mathbf{S}^{\nu}_{(\alpha,\beta)}\}
; in particular, since
(\partial F)\setminus W
is a minimal surface and
W\subset\textbf{{C}}_{d/2}^{\nu}(x_{0})
, by analytic continuation we find that

(\partial F)\setminus\textbf{{C}}_{d/2}^{\nu}(x_{0})=\Pi\setminus\textbf{{C}}_{d/2}^{\nu}(x_{0})\,,\qquad\Pi=\big\{x:x\cdot\nu=a\big\}\,.

(4.26)

By (
4.26
), we have that for
R>R^{\prime}
,

P(F;\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu}\setminus W)-\omega_{n}\,R^{n}=P(F;\textbf{{C}}_{d/2}^{\nu}(x_{0})\setminus W)-\omega_{n}\,(d/2)^{n}\,.

Going back to (
4.22
), this implies
P(F;\textbf{{C}}_{d/2}^{\nu}(x_{0})\setminus W)=0
. However, since
(\partial F)\setminus W
is (distributionally) a minimal surface,
P(F;B_{\rho}(x)\setminus W)\geq\omega_{n}\,\rho^{n}
whenever
x\in(\partial F)\setminus W
and
\rho<{\rm dist}(x,W)
, so that

P(F;\textbf{{C}}_{d/2}^{\nu}(x_{0})\setminus W)=0
gives
((\partial F)\setminus W)\cap\textbf{{C}}_{d/2}^{\nu}(x_{0})=\emptyset
. Hence, using also (
4.26
), we find
(\partial F)\setminus W=\Pi\setminus\mathrm{cl}\,\big(B_{d/2}(x)\big)
for some
x\in\Pi
, that is (
1.16
).

Step eight:
We finally prove that
\mathcal{R}(W)=\omega_{n}\,(d/2)^{n}
if and only if there are a hyperplane
\Pi
and a point
x\in\Pi
such that

\displaystyle\Pi\cap\partial B_{d/2}(x)\subset W\,,

(4.27)

\displaystyle\mbox{$\Omega\setminus(\Pi\setminus B_{d/2}(x))$ has two unbounded connected components}\,.

(4.28)

We first prove that the two conditions are sufficient. Let
\nu
be a unit normal to
\Pi
and let
\Pi^{+}
and
\Pi^{-}
be the two open half-spaces bounded by
\Pi
. The condition
\Pi\cup\partial B_{d/2}(x)\subset W
implies
W\subset\textbf{{C}}_{d/2}^{\nu}(x)
, and thus

\Omega\setminus\mathrm{cl}\,\big[\textbf{{C}}^{\nu}_{d/2,(-d,d)}(x)\big]=(\Pi^{+}\cup\Pi^{-})\setminus\mathrm{cl}\,\big[\textbf{{C}}^{\nu}_{d/2,(-d,d)}(x)\big]\,.

In particular,
\Omega\setminus(\Pi\setminus B_{d/2}(x))
has a connected component
F
which contains

\Pi^{+}\setminus\mathrm{cl}\,\big[\textbf{{C}}^{\nu}_{d/2,(-d,d)}(x)\big]\,;

and since
\Omega\setminus(\Pi\setminus B_{d/2}(x))
contains exactly two unbounded connected components, it cannot be that
F
contains also
\Pi^{-}\setminus\mathrm{cl}\,[\textbf{{C}}^{\nu}_{d/2,(-d,d)}(x)]
, therefore

\Pi^{+}\setminus\mathrm{cl}\,\big[\textbf{{C}}^{\nu}_{d/2,(-d,d)}(x)\big]\subset F\,,\qquad\Pi^{-}\setminus\mathrm{cl}\,\big[\textbf{{C}}^{\nu}_{d/2,(-d,d)}(x)\big]\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus\mathrm{cl}\,(F)\,.

(4.29)

As a consequence
\partial F
is contained in the slab
\{y:|(y-x)\cdot\nu|<d\}
, and is such that
\mathbf{p}_{\nu^{\perp}}(\partial F)=\nu^{\perp}
, that is,
(F,\nu)\in\mathcal{F}
. Moreover, (
4.29
) implies

\Pi\setminus\mathrm{cl}\,(B_{d/2}(x))\subset\Omega\cap\partial F\,,

while the fact that
F
is a connected component of
\Omega\setminus(\Pi\setminus B_{d/2}(x))
implies
\Omega\cap\partial F\subset\Pi\setminus\mathrm{cl}\,(B_{d/2}(x))
. In conclusion,
\Omega\cap\partial F=\Pi\setminus\mathrm{cl}\,(B_{d/2}(x))
, hence

\omega_{n}\,(d/2)^{n}=\lim_{r\to\infty}\omega_{n}r^{n}-P(F;\textbf{C}_{r}^{\nu}\setminus W)\leq\mathcal{R}(W)\leq\omega_{n}\,(d/2)^{n}\,,

(4.30)

and
\mathcal{R}(W)=\omega_{n}\,(d/2)^{n}
, as claimed. We prove that the two conditions are necessary. Let
(F,\nu)\in{\rm Max}[\mathcal{R}(W)]
. As proved in step seven, there is a hyperplane
\Pi
and
x\in\Pi
such that
\Omega\cap\partial F=\Pi\setminus\mathrm{cl}\,(B_{d/2}(x))
. If
z\in\Pi\cap\partial B_{d/2}(x)
but
z\in\Omega
, then there is
\rho>0
such that
B_{\rho}(z)\subset\Omega
, and since
\partial F
is a minimal surface in
\Omega
, we would obtain that
\Pi\cap B_{\rho}(z)\subset\Omega\cap\partial F
, against
\Omega\cap\partial F=\Pi\setminus\mathrm{cl}\,(B_{d/2}(x))
. So it must be
\Pi\cap\partial B_{d/2}(x)\subset W
, and the necessity of (
4.27
) is proved. To prove the necessity of (
4.28
), we notice that since
\Pi^{+}\setminus\mathrm{cl}\,[\textbf{{C}}^{\nu}_{d/2,(-d,d)}(x)]
and
\Pi^{-}\setminus\mathrm{cl}\,[\textbf{{C}}^{\nu}_{d/2,(-d,d)}(x)]
are both open, connected, and unbounded subsets of
\Omega\setminus(\Pi\setminus B_{d/2}(x))
, and since the complement in
\Omega\setminus(\Pi\setminus B_{d/2}(x))
of their union is bounded, it must be that
\Omega\setminus(\Pi\setminus B_{d/2}(x))
has
at most
two unbounded connected components: therefore we just need to exclude that
it has only one
. Assuming by contradiction that this is the case, we could then connect any point
x^{+}\in\Pi^{+}\setminus\mathrm{cl}\,[\textbf{{C}}^{\nu}_{d/2,(-d,d)}(x)]
to any point
x^{-}\in\Pi^{-}\setminus\mathrm{cl}\,[\textbf{{C}}^{\nu}_{d/2,(-d,d)}(x)]
with a continuous path
\gamma
entirely contained in
\Omega\setminus(\Pi\setminus B_{d/2}(x))
. Now, recalling that
\Omega\cap\partial F=\Pi\setminus\mathrm{cl}\,(B_{d/2}(x))
, we can pick
x_{0}\in\Pi\setminus\mathrm{cl}\,(B_{d/2}(x))
and
r>0
so that

B_{r}(x_{0})\cap\Pi^{+}\subset F\,,\qquad B_{r}(x_{0})\cap\Pi^{-}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus\mathrm{cl}\,(F)\,,

(4.31)

and
B_{r}(x_{0})\cap\mathrm{cl}\,[\textbf{{C}}^{\nu}_{d/2,(-d,d)}(x)]=\emptyset
. We can then pick
x^{+}\in B_{r}(x_{0})\cap\Pi^{+}
,
x^{-}\in B_{r}(x_{0})\cap\Pi^{-}
, and then connect them by a path
\gamma
entirely contained in
\Omega\setminus(\Pi\setminus B_{d/2}(x))
. By (
4.31
),
\gamma
must intersect
\partial F
, and since
\gamma
is contained in
\Omega
, we see that
\gamma
must intersect
\Omega\cap\partial F=\Pi\setminus\mathrm{cl}\,(B_{d/2}(x))
, which of course contradicts the containment of
\gamma
in
\Omega\setminus(\Pi\setminus B_{d/2}(x))
. We have thus proved that
\Omega\setminus(\Pi\setminus B_{d/2}(x))
has exactly two unbounded connected components.
∎

5.
Resolution theorem for exterior isoperimetric sets

The notation set in (
4.1
) is in use. Given
v_{j}\to\infty
, we set
\lambda_{j}=v_{j}^{1/(n+1)}
.

Proof of Theorem
1.6
.

We recall that, throughout the proof,
\mathcal{R}(W)>0
. Theorem
1.6
-(i) and the estimate for
|v^{-1/(n+1)}\,|x|-\omega_{n+1}^{-1/(n+1)}|
in Theorem
1.6
-(iv), have already been proved in Theorem
3.1
-(ii, iii).

Step one:
We prove that

\varlimsup_{v\to\infty}\psi_{W}(v)-P(B^{(v)})\leq-\mathcal{R}(W)\,.

(5.1)

To this end, let
(F,\nu)\in{\rm Max}[\mathcal{R}(W)]
, so that by (
1.13
) and (
1.14
), we have

F\setminus\textbf{{C}}_{R_{2}}^{\nu}=\big\{x+t\,\nu:x\in\nu^{\perp}\,,|x|>R_{2}\,,t<f(x)\big\}\,,

(5.2)

for a function
f\in C^{1}(\nu^{\perp})
satisfying

\displaystyle\big|f(x)-\big(a+b\,|x|^{2-n}+(c\cdot x)\,|x|^{-n}\big)\big|\leq C_{0}\,|x|^{-n}\,,

(5.3)

\displaystyle\max\big\{|x|^{n-1}\,|\nabla f(x)|,|x|^{n}\,|\nabla^{2}f(x)|\big\}\leq C_{0}\,,\qquad\forall x\in\nu^{\perp}\,,|x|>R_{2}\,,

and for some
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
and
c\in\nu^{\perp}
such that
\max\{|a|,|b|,|c|\}\leq C(W)<\infty
(moreover, we can take
b=0
,
c=0
and
C_{0}=0
if
n=1
). We are going to construct competitors for
\psi_{W}(v)
with
v
large by gluing a large sphere
S
to
\partial F
along
\partial\textbf{{C}}_{r}^{\nu}
for
r>R_{2}
. This operation comes at the price of an area error located on the cylinder
\partial\textbf{{C}}_{r}^{\nu}
. We can make this error negligible thanks to the fact that (
5.3
) determines the distance (inside of
\partial\textbf{{C}}_{r}^{\nu}
) of
\partial F
from a hyperplane (namely,
\partial G_{r}
for the half-space
G_{r}
defined below) up to
{\rm o}(r^{1-n})
as
r\to\infty
. Thus, the asymptotic expansion (
1.14
) is just as precise as needed in order to perform this construction, i.e. our construction would not be possible with a less precise information.

We now discuss the construction in detail. Given
r>R_{2}
, we consider the half-space
G_{r}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
defined by the condition that

G_{r}\cap\partial\textbf{{C}}_{r}^{\nu}=\big\{x+t\,\nu:x\in\nu^{\perp}\,,|x|=r\,,t<a+b\,r^{2-n}+(c\cdot x)\,r^{-n}\big\}\,,

(5.4)

so that
G_{r}
is the “best half-space approximation” of
F
on
\partial\textbf{{C}}_{r}^{\nu}
according to (
5.3
). Denoting by
\mathrm{hd}\,(X,Y)
the Hausdorff distance between
X,Y\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
, for every
r>R_{2}
and
v>0
we can define
x_{r,v}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
in such a way that
v\mapsto x_{r,v}
is continuous and

\displaystyle\lim_{v\to\infty}\mathrm{hd}\,(B^{(v)}(x_{r,v})\cap K,G_{r}\cap K)=0\qquad\forall\,K\subset\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\,.

(5.5)

Thus, the balls
B^{(v)}(x_{r,v})
have volume
v
and are locally converging in Hausdorff distance, as
v\to\infty
, to the optimal half-space
G_{r}
. Finally, we notice that by (
5.3
) we can find
\alpha<\beta
such that

\displaystyle\big((\partial F)\cup(\partial G_{r})\cup(G_{r}\Delta F)\big)\cap\textbf{{C}}_{r}^{\nu}\,\,\subset\,\,\textbf{{C}}_{r,(\alpha+1,\beta-1)}^{\nu}\,,

(5.6)

and then define
F_{r,v}
by setting

F_{r,v}=\big(F\cap\textbf{{C}}_{r,(\alpha,\beta)}^{\nu}\big)\cup\big(B^{(v)}(x_{r,v})\setminus\mathrm{cl}\,\big[\textbf{{C}}_{r,(\alpha,\beta)}^{\nu}\big]\big)\,,

(5.7)

see

C
C

Figure 5.1.
The competitors
F_{r,v}
constructed in (
5.7
). A maximizer
F
in the isoperimetric residue
\mathcal{R}(W)
is joined to a ball of volume
v
, whose center
x_{r,v}
is determined by looking at best hyperplane
\partial G_{r}
approximating
\partial F
on the “lateral” cylinder
\partial\textbf{{C}}_{r}^{\nu}
. To ensure the area error made in joining this large sphere to
\partial F
is negligible, the distance between
\partial F
and the sphere inside
\partial\textbf{{C}}_{r}^{\nu}
must be
{\rm o}(r^{1-n})
as
r\to\infty
. The asymptotic expansion (
5.3
) gives a hyperplane
\partial G_{r}
which is close to
\partial F
up to
{\rm O}(r^{-n})
, and is thus just as precise as needed to perform the construction.

Figure
5.1
. We claim that, by using
F_{r,v}
as comparisons for
\psi_{W}(|F_{r,v}|)
, and then sending first
v\to\infty
and then
r\to\infty
, one obtains (
5.1
). We first notice that by (
5.5
) and (
5.6
) (see, e.g.
[
Mag12
, Theorem 16.16]
), we have

\displaystyle P(F_{r,v};\Omega)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P(F;\textbf{{C}}_{r,(\alpha,\beta)}^{\nu}\setminus W)+P\big(B^{(v)}(x_{r,v});\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus\mathrm{cl}\,\big[\textbf{{C}}_{r,(\alpha,\beta)}^{\nu}\big]\big)

(5.8)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{H}^{n}\big((F\Delta B^{(v)}(x_{r,v}))\cap\partial_{\ell}\textbf{{C}}_{r,(\alpha,\beta)}^{\nu}\big)\,,

where the last term is the “gluing error” generated by the mismatch between the boundaries of
\partial F
and
\partial B^{(v)}(x_{r,v})
along
\partial_{\ell}\textbf{{C}}_{r,(\alpha,\beta)}^{\nu}
. Now, thanks to (
5.3
) we have

\mathrm{hd}\,(G_{r}\cap\partial\textbf{{C}}_{r}^{\nu},F\cap\partial\textbf{{C}}_{r}^{\nu})\leq C_{0}\,r^{-n}
, so that

\mathcal{H}^{n}\big((F\Delta G_{r})\cap\partial\textbf{{C}}_{r}^{\nu}\big)\leq n\,\omega_{n}\,r^{n-1}\,\mathrm{hd}\,(G_{r}\cap\partial\textbf{{C}}_{r}^{\nu},F\cap\partial\textbf{{C}}_{r}^{\nu})\leq C(n,W)/r\,.

(5.9)

At the same time, by (
5.5
),

\lim_{v\to\infty}\mathcal{H}^{n}\big((G_{r}\Delta B^{(v)}(x_{r,v}))\cap\partial_{\ell}\textbf{{C}}_{r,(\alpha,\beta)}^{\nu}\big)=0\,,

(5.10)

and thus we have the following estimate for the gluing error,

\varlimsup_{v\to\infty}\mathcal{H}^{n}\big((F\Delta B^{(v)}(x_{r,v}))\cap\partial_{\ell}\textbf{{C}}_{r,(\alpha,\beta)}^{\nu}\big)\leq\frac{C(n,W)}{r}\,,\qquad\forall r>R_{2}\,.

(5.11)

Again by (
5.5
), we find

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\lim_{v\to\infty}P\big(B^{(v)}(x_{r,v});\textbf{{C}}_{r,(\alpha,\beta)}^{\nu}\big)=P\big(G_{r};\textbf{{C}}_{r,(\alpha,\beta)}^{\nu}\big)

(5.12)

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!1\leq(\omega_{n}\,r^{n})^{-1}\,P\big(G_{r};\textbf{{C}}_{r,(\alpha,\beta)}^{\nu}\big)=\fint_{\textbf{{D}}_{r}^{\nu}}\sqrt{1+(c/r^{n})^{2}}\leq 1+C_{0}\,r^{-2\,n}\,,

(5.13)

so that, by (
5.12
) and by the lower bound in (
5.13
), for every
r>R_{2}
,

\varlimsup_{v\to\infty}P\big(B^{(v)}(x_{r,v});\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus\mathrm{cl}\,\big[\textbf{{C}}_{r,(\alpha,\beta)}^{\nu}\big]\big)-P(B^{(v)})\leq-\omega_{n}\,r^{n}\,.

(5.14)

Combining (
5.11
) and (
5.14
) with (
5.8
) and the fact that
\textbf{{C}}_{r,(\alpha,\beta)}^{\nu}\cap\partial F=\textbf{{C}}_{r}^{\nu}\cap\partial F
(see (
5.6
)), we find that for every
r>R_{2}
,

\displaystyle\varlimsup_{v\to\infty}P(F_{r,v};\Omega)-P(B^{(v)})\leq P(F;\textbf{{C}}_{r}^{\nu}\setminus W)-\omega_{n}\,r^{n}+C(n,W)/r

\displaystyle\leq-{\rm res}_{W}(F,\nu)+C(n,W)/r=-\mathcal{R}(W)+C(n,W)/r\,.

(5.15)

where (
4.9
) has been used. Now, combining the elementary estimates

\max\big\{\big||F_{r,v}|-v\big|\,,v^{-1/(n+1)}\,|P(B^{(v)})-P(B^{(|F_{r,v}|)})|\big\}\leq C(n)\,r^{n+1}

(5.16)

with (
5.15
), we see that

\displaystyle\varlimsup_{v\to\infty}\psi_{W}(|F_{r,v}|)-P(B^{(|F_{r,v}|)})\leq-\mathcal{R}(W)+C(n,W)/r\,,\,\,\forall r>R_{2}\,.

(5.17)

Again by (
5.16
) and since
v\mapsto|F_{r,v}|
is a continuous function, we see that

\varlimsup_{v\to\infty}\psi_{W}(|F_{r,v}|)-P(B^{(|F_{r,v}|)})=\varlimsup_{v\to\infty}\psi_{W}(v)-P(B^{(v)})
. This last identity combined with (
5.17
) implies (
5.1
) in the limit
r\to\infty
.

Step two:
Now let
E_{j}\in{\rm Min}[\psi_{W}(v_{j})]
for
v_{j}\to\infty
. By (
3.1
) and a standard argument (see, e.g.
[
Mag12
, Theorem 21.14]
), there is a local perimeter minimizer with free boundary
F
in
\Omega
such that, up to extracting subsequences,

\displaystyle\mbox{$E_{j}\to F$ in $L^{1}_{{\rm loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})$, $\mathcal{H}^{n}\llcorner\partial E_{j}\rightharpoonup\mathcal{H}^{n}\llcorner\partial F$ as Radon measures in $\Omega$}\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{hd}\,(K\cap\partial E_{j};K\cap\partial F)\to 0\qquad\mbox{for every $K\subset\subset\Omega$}\,.

(5.18)

Notice that it is not immediate to conclude from
E_{j}\in{\rm Min}[\psi_{W}(v_{j})]
that (for some
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{n}
)
(F,\nu)\in{\rm Max}[\mathcal{R}(W)]
(or even that
(\nu,F)\in\mathcal{F}
), nor that
P(E_{j};\Omega)-P(B^{(v_{j})})
is asymptotically bounded from below by
-{\rm res}_{W}(F,\nu)
. In this step we prove some preliminary properties of
F
, and in particular we exploit the blowdown result for exterior minimal surfaces contained in Theorem
2.1
-(ii) to prove that
F
satisfies (
5.2
) and (
5.3
) (see statement (c) below). Then, in step three, we use the decay rates (
5.3
) to show that
E_{j}
can be “glued” to
F
, similarly to the construction of step one, and then derive from the corresponding energy estimates the lower bound matching (
5.1
) and the optimality of
F
in
\mathcal{R}(W)
.

(a)

\Omega\cap\partial F\cap\partial B_{\rho}\neq\emptyset
for every
\rho
such that
W\subset\subset B_{\rho}
: If not there would be
\varepsilon>0
such that
W\subset\subset B_{\rho-\varepsilon}
and
\Omega\cap\partial F\cap A_{\rho-\varepsilon}^{\rho+\varepsilon}=\emptyset
(recall that
A_{r}^{s}=\{x:s>|x|>r\}
). By (
5.18
) and the constant mean curvature condition satisfied by
\Omega\cap\partial E_{j}
, we would then find that each
E_{j}
(with
j
large enough) has a connected component of the form
B^{(w_{j})}(x_{j})
, with
B^{(w_{j})}(x_{j})\subset\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus B_{\rho+\varepsilon}
and
w_{j}\geq v_{j}-C(n)\,(\rho+\varepsilon)^{n+1}
. In particular, against
\mathcal{R}(W)>0
,

\psi_{W}(v_{j})=P(E_{j};\Omega)\geq P(B^{(v_{j}-C\,(\rho+\varepsilon)^{n+1})})\geq P(B^{(v_{j})})-C\lambda_{j}^{-1}(\rho+\varepsilon)^{n+1}\,.

(b)

Sharp area bound
: We combine the upper energy bound (
5.1
) with the perimeter inequality for spherical symmetrization, to prove

P(F;\Omega\cap B_{r})\leq\omega_{n}r^{n}-\mathcal{R}(W)\,,\qquad\mbox{for every $r$ s.t. $W\subset\subset B_{r}$}\,.

(5.19)

(Notice that (
5.19
) does not immediately imply the bound for
P(F;\Omega\cap\textbf{{C}}_{r}^{\nu})
which would be needed to compare
\mathcal{R}(W)
and
{\rm res}_{W}(F,\nu)
.) To prove (
5.19
) we argue by contradiction, and consider the existence of
\delta>0
and
r
with
W\subset\subset B_{r}
such that

P(F;\Omega\cap\ B_{r})\geq\omega_{n}\,r^{n}-\mathcal{R}(W)+\delta
. In particular, for
j
large enough, we would then have

P(E_{j};\Omega\cap B_{r})\geq\omega_{n}r^{n}-\mathcal{R}(W)+\delta\,.

(5.20)

Again for
j
large, it must be
\mathcal{H}^{n}(\partial E_{j}\cap\partial B_{r})=0
: indeed, by (
3.1
),
\Omega\cap\partial E_{j}
has mean curvature of order
{\rm O}(\lambda_{j}^{-1})
, while of course
\partial B_{r}
has constant mean curvature equal to
n/r
. Thanks to
\mathcal{H}^{n}(\partial E_{j}\cap\partial B_{r})=0
,

P(E_{j};\Omega)=P(E_{j};\Omega\cap B_{r})+P\big(E_{j};\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus\mathrm{cl}\,(B_{r})\big)\,.

(5.21)

If
E_{j}^{s}
denotes the spherical symmetral of
E_{j}
such that
E_{j}^{s}\cap\partial B_{\rho}
is a spherical cap in
\partial B_{\rho}
, centered at
\rho\,e_{n+1}
, with area equal to
\mathcal{H}^{n}(E_{j}\cap\partial B_{\rho})
, then we have the perimeter inequality

P\big(E_{j};\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus\mathrm{cl}\,(B_{r})\big)\geq P\big(E_{j}^{s};\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus\mathrm{cl}\,(B_{r})\big)\,;

(5.22)

see
[
CPS20
]
. Now, we can find a half-space
J
orthogonal to
e_{n+1}
and such that
\mathcal{H}^{n}(J\cap\partial B_{r})=\mathcal{H}^{n}(E_{j}\cap\partial B_{r})
. In this way, using that
|E_{j}^{s}\setminus B_{r}|=|E_{j}\setminus B_{r}|
(by Fubini’s theorem in spherical coordinates), and that
\mathcal{H}^{n}(B_{r}\cap\partial J)\leq\omega_{n}\,r^{n}
(by the fact that
\partial J
is a hyperplane), we find

\displaystyle P\big(E_{j}^{s};\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus\mathrm{cl}\,(B_{r})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P\big((E_{j}^{s}\setminus\mathrm{cl}\,(B_{r}))\cup(J\cap B_{r})\big)-\mathcal{H}^{n}(B_{r}\cap\partial J)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle P\big(B^{(|E_{j}|-|E_{j}\cap B_{r}|+|J\cap B_{r}|)}\big)-\omega_{n}\,r^{n}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle P(B^{(v_{j})})-C(n)\,r^{n+1}\,\lambda_{j}^{-1}-\omega_{n}\,r^{n}

which, with (
5.20
), (
5.21
) and (
5.22
), finally gives

P(E_{j};\Omega)-P(B^{(v_{j})})>-\mathcal{R}(W)+\delta-C(n)\,r^{n+1}\,\lambda_{j}^{-1}

for
j
large, against (
5.1
).

(c)

Asymptotic behavior of
\partial F
: We prove that there are
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{n}
,
f\in C^{\infty}(\nu^{\perp})
,
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
,
c\in\nu^{\perp}
,
R^{\prime}>\sup\{\rho:W\subset\textbf{{C}}_{\rho}^{\nu}\}
and
C
positive, with

\displaystyle\partial F\setminus\textbf{{C}}^{\nu}_{R^{\prime}}=\big\{x+f(x)\,\nu:x\in\nu^{\perp}\,,|x|>R^{\prime}\big\}\,,

(5.23)

\displaystyle f(x)=a\,,\hskip 187.78836pt(n=1)

\displaystyle\big|f(x)-\big(a+b\,|x|^{2-n}+(c\cdot x)\,|x|^{-n}\big)\big|\leq C\,|x|^{-n}\,,\,\,\,(n\geq 2)\,,

(5.24)

\displaystyle\max\big\{|x|^{n-1}\,|\nabla f(x)|,|x|^{n}\,|\nabla^{2}f(x)|\big\}\leq C_{0}\,,\qquad\forall x\in\nu^{\perp}\,,|x|>R^{\prime}\,.

To this end, by a standard argument exploiting the local perimeter minimality of
F
in
\Omega
, given
r_{j}\to\infty
, then, up to extracting subsequences,
F/r_{j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm loc}}}{{\to}}J
in
L^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})
, where
J
is a perimeter minimizer in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus\{0\}
,
0\in\partial J
(thanks to property (a)),
J
is a cone with vertex at
0

(thanks to Theorem
2.7
and, in particular to (
2.47
)), and
P(J;B_{1})\leq\omega_{n}
(by (
5.19
)).
If
n\geq 2
, then
\partial J
has vanishing distributional mean curvature in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
(as points are removable singularities for the mean curvature operator when
n\geq 2
), thus
P(J;B_{1})\geq\omega_{n}
by upper semicontinuity of area densities, and, finally, by
P(J;B_{1})=\omega_{n}
and Allard’s regularity theorem,
J
is a half-space.
If
n=1
, then
\partial J
is the union of two half-lines
\ell_{1}
and
\ell_{2}
meeting at
\{0\}
. If
\ell_{1}
and
\ell_{2}
are not opposite (i.e., if
J
is not a half-space), then we can find a half-space
J^{*}
such that
(J\cap J^{*})\Delta J\subset\subset B\subset\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\{0\}
for some ball
B
, and
P(J\cap J^{*};B)<P(J;B)
, thus violating the fact that
J
is a perimeter minimizer in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus\{0\}
.

If
n=1
it is immediate from the above information that, for some
R^{\prime}>0
,
F\setminus B_{R^{\prime}}=J\setminus B_{R^{\prime}}
; this proves (
5.23
) and (
5.24
) in the case
n=1
. To prove (
5.23
) and (
5.24
) when
n\geq 2
, we let
M_{0}
and
\varepsilon_{0}
be as in Theorem
2.1
-(ii) with
(n,\Gamma=2\,n\,\omega_{n},\sigma=1)
. Since
J
is a half-space, by using Remark
3.2
and
F/r_{j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm loc}}}{{\to}}J
on the annulus
A_{1/2}^{2\,L}
, for some
L>\max\{M_{0},64\}
to be chosen later on depending also on
\varepsilon_{0}
, we find that

(\partial F)\cap A^{4\,L\,r_{j}}_{r_{j}/2}=\big\{x+r_{j}\,f_{j}\big(x/r_{j}\big)\,\nu:x\in\nu^{\perp}\big\}\cap A^{4\,L\,r_{j}}_{r_{j}/2}\,,\qquad\nu^{\perp}=\partial J\,,

(5.25)

for
f_{j}\in C^{1}(\nu^{\perp})
with
\|f_{j}\|_{C^{1}(\nu^{\perp})}\to 0
. By (
5.25
),

V_{j}=\mathbf{var}\,\big((\partial F)\setminus B_{r_{j}},1)\in\mathcal{V}_{n}(0,r_{j},\infty)
, with (for
{\rm o}(1)\to 0
as
j\to\infty
)

\displaystyle r_{j}^{-n}\,\int x\cdot\nu^{\rm co}_{V_{j}}\,d{\rm bd}_{V_{j}}=-n\,\omega_{n}+{\rm o}(1)

(5.26)

\displaystyle r_{j}^{1-n}\|{\rm bd}_{V_{j}}\|(\partial B_{r_{j}})=n\,\omega_{n}+{\rm o}(1)\,,

(5.27)

\displaystyle\sup_{r\in(r_{j},3\,L\,r_{j})}\big|(r^{n}-r_{j}^{n})^{-1}\,\|V_{j}\|(B_{r}\setminus B_{r_{j}})-\omega_{n}\big|={\rm o}(1)\,.

(5.28)

By our choice of
\Gamma
, by (
5.19
) and (
5.26
) we see that, for
j
large, we have

\|{\rm bd}_{V_{j}}\|(\partial B_{r_{j}})\leq\Gamma\,r_{j}^{n-1}\,,\qquad\|V_{j}\|(B_{\rho}\setminus B_{r_{j}})\leq\Gamma\rho^{n}\,,\,\,\forall\rho>r_{j}\,.

(5.29)

Moreover, we claim that setting

s_{j}=2\,L\,r_{j}

(so that, in particular,
s_{j}>\max\{M_{0},64\}\,r_{j}
), then

\displaystyle|\delta_{V_{j},r_{j},0}(s_{j}/8)|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\,,\qquad\inf_{r>s_{j}/8}\delta_{V_{j},r_{j},0}(r)\geq-\varepsilon_{0}\,,

(5.30)

provided
j
and
L
are taken large enough depending on
\varepsilon_{0}
. To check the first inequality in (
5.30
) we notice that, by (
5.26
) and (
5.28
),

\displaystyle\delta_{V_{j},r_{j},0}(s_{j}/8)\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\omega_{n}-\frac{\|V_{j}\|(B_{s_{j}/8}\setminus B_{r_{j}})}{(s_{j}/8)^{n}}+\frac{1}{n\,(s_{j}/8)^{n}}\,\int x\cdot\nu^{\rm co}_{V_{j}}\,d\,{\rm bd}_{V_{j}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\omega_{n}-\big(\omega_{n}+{\rm o}(1)\big)\,\frac{(s_{j}/8)^{n}-r_{j}^{n}}{(s_{j}/8)^{n}}-\frac{\omega_{n}\,r_{j}^{n}}{(s_{j}/8)^{n}}\,\big(1+{\rm o}(1)\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!{\rm o}(1)\,(1+(r_{j}/s_{j})^{n})={\rm o}(1)\,,

so that
|\delta_{V_{j},r_{j},0}(s_{j}/8)|\leq\varepsilon_{0}
as soon as
j
is large with respect to
\varepsilon_{0}
. Similarly, if
r>s_{j}/8=(L\,r_{j})/4
, then by (
5.26
), (
5.28
), (
5.19
), and
r_{j}/r\leq 4/L
,

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\delta_{V_{j},r_{j},0}(r)=\omega_{n}-\frac{\|V_{j}\|(B_{r}\setminus B_{2\,r_{j}})}{r^{n}}-\frac{\|V_{j}\|(B_{2\,r_{j}}\setminus B_{r_{j}})}{r^{n}}-\frac{\omega_{n}\,r_{j}^{n}}{r^{n}}\,\big(1+{\rm o}(1)\big)

\displaystyle\geq\omega_{n}-\frac{\omega_{n}\,r^{n}-\mathcal{R}(W)}{r^{n}}-\big(\omega_{n}+{\rm o}(1)\big)\,\frac{(2\,r_{j})^{n}-r_{j}^{n}}{r^{n}}-\frac{\omega_{n}\,r_{j}^{n}}{r^{n}}\,\big(1+{\rm o}(1)\big)

\displaystyle\geq r^{-n}\,\mathcal{R}(W)-2\,(4/L)^{n}\,\big(\omega_{n}+{\rm o}(1)\big)-(4/L)^{n}\,{\rm o}(1)\geq-3\,(4/L)^{n}\,\omega_{n}\,,

provided
j
is large; hence the second inequality in (
5.30
) holds if
L
is large in terms of
\varepsilon_{0}
.
By (
5.29
) and (
5.30
), Theorem
2.1
-(ii) can be applied to
(V,R,\Lambda,s)=(V_{j},r_{j},0,s_{j})
with
j
large. As a consequence, passing from spherical graphs to cylindrical graphs with the aid of Lemma
B.1
, we find that, for some large
j
,

(\partial F)\setminus B_{s_{j}/16}=\big\{x+f(x)\,\nu:x\in\nu^{\perp}\big\}\setminus B_{s_{j}/16}\,,

(5.31)

where
f:\nu^{\perp}\to\mathbb{R}
is a smooth function which solves the minimal surfaces equation on
\nu^{\perp}\setminus B_{s_{j}/16}
. Since
\partial F
admits at least one sequential blowdown limit hyperplane (namely,
\nu^{\perp}=\partial J
), by a theorem of Simon
[
Sim87
, Theorem 2]
we find that
\nabla f
has a limit as
|x|\to\infty
; in particular,
|\nabla f|
is bounded. Moreover, by (
5.31
) (or by the fact that
F
is a local perimeter minimizer in
\Omega
),
\partial F
is a stable minimal surface in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus B_{s_{j}/16}
, which, thanks to (
5.19
), satisfies an area growth bound like (
4.3
). We can thus apply Proposition
4.1
to deduce the validity of (
5.24
) when
n\geq 3
, and of
|f(x)-[a+b\,\log\,|x|+(c\cdot x)\,|x|^{-2}]|\leq C\,|x|^{-2}
for all
|x|>R^{\prime}
when
n=2
(with
R^{\prime}>s_{j}
). Recalling that
F
is a local perimeter minimizer with free boundary in
\Omega
(that is,
P(F;\Omega\cap B)\leq P(F^{\prime};\Omega\cap B)
whenever
F\Delta F^{\prime}\subset\subset B\subset\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
) it must be that
b=0
, as it can be seen by comparing
F
with the set
F^{\prime}
obtained by changing
F
inside
\textbf{{C}}_{r}^{\nu}
(
r>>R^{\prime}
) with the half-space
G_{r}
bounded by the plane
\{x+t\,\nu:x\in\nu^{\perp},t=a+b\,\log(r)+c\cdot x/r^{2}\}
and such that
\mathcal{H}^{2}((F\Delta G_{r})\cap\partial\textbf{{C}}_{r}^{\nu})\leq C/r^{2}
(we omit the details of this standard comparison argument). Having shown that
b=0
, the proof is complete.

(d)

F\cup W
defines an element of
\mathcal{F}
: With
R>R^{\prime}
as in (
5.23
) and (
5.24
),
V_{R}=\mathbf{var}\,((\partial F)\cap(B_{R}\setminus W))
is a stationary varifold in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus K_{R}
for

K_{R}=W\cup\big\{x+f(x)\,\nu:x\in\nu^{\perp}\,,|x|=R\}
, and has bounded support. By the convex hull property
[
Sim83b
, Theorem 19.2]
, we deduce that, for every
R>R^{\prime}
,
{\rm spt}V_{R}
is contained in the convex hull of
K_{R}
, for every
R>R^{\prime}
. Taking into account that
f(x)\to a
as
|x|\to\infty
we conclude that
\Omega\cap\partial F
is contained in the smallest slab
\mathbf{S}_{[\alpha,\beta]}^{\nu}
containing both
W
and
\{x:x\cdot\nu=a\}
. Now set

F^{\prime}=F\cup W
. Clearly
F^{\prime}
is a set of locally finite perimeter in
\Omega
(since
P(F^{\prime};\Omega^{\prime})=P(F;\Omega^{\prime})
for every
\Omega^{\prime}\subset\subset\Omega
). Second,
\partial F^{\prime}
is contained in
\mathbf{S}_{[\alpha,\beta]}^{\nu}
(since
\partial F^{\prime}\subset[(\partial F)\cap\Omega]\cup W
). Third, by (
5.23
) and (
5.24
),

\displaystyle\big\{x+t\,\nu:x\in\nu^{\perp}\,,|x|>R^{\prime}\,,t<\alpha\big\}\subset F^{\prime}\,,

(5.32)

\displaystyle\big\{x+t\,\nu:x\in\nu^{\perp}\,,|x|>R^{\prime}\,,t>\beta\big\}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus F^{\prime}\,,

(5.33)

\displaystyle\big\{x+t\,\nu:x\in\nu^{\perp}\,,|x|<R^{\prime}\,,t\in\mathbb{R}\setminus[\alpha,\beta]\big\}\cap(\partial F^{\prime})=\emptyset\,.

(5.34)

By combining (
5.32
) and (
5.34
) we see that
\{x+t\,\nu:x\in\nu^{\perp}\,,t<\alpha\}\subset F^{\prime}
, and by combining (
5.33
) and (
5.34
) we see that
\{x+t\,\nu:x\in\nu^{\perp}\,,t>\beta\}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus F^{\prime}
: in particular,
\mathbf{p}_{\nu^{\perp}}(\partial F^{\prime})=\nu^{\perp}
, and thus
(F^{\prime},\nu)\in\mathcal{F}
.

Step three:
We prove that

\varliminf_{v\to\infty}\psi_{W}(v)-P(B^{(v)})\geq-\mathcal{R}(W)\,.

(5.35)

For
v_{j}\to\infty
achieving the liminf in (
5.35
), let
E_{j}\in{\rm Min}[\psi_{W}(v_{j})]
and let
F
be a (sub-sequential) limit of
E_{j}
, so that properties (a), (b), (c) and (d) in step two hold for
F
. In particular, properties (
5.23
) and (
5.24
) from (c) are entirely analogous to properties (
5.2
) and (
5.3
) exploited in step one: therefore, the family of half-spaces
\{G_{r}\}_{r>R^{\prime}}
defined by (
5.4
) is such that

\displaystyle\big((\partial F)\cup(\partial G_{r})\cup(G_{r}\Delta F)\big)\cap\textbf{{C}}_{r}^{\nu}\,\,\subset\,\,\textbf{{C}}_{r,(\alpha+1,\beta-1)}^{\nu}\,,

(5.36)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{n}\big((F\Delta G_{r})\cap\partial\textbf{{C}}_{r}^{\nu}\big)\leq r^{-1}\,C(n,W)\,,

(5.37)

\displaystyle\big|P\big(G_{r};\textbf{{C}}_{r,(\alpha,\beta)}^{\nu}\big)-\omega_{n}\,r^{n}\big|\leq r^{-n}\,C(n,W)\,,

(5.38)

(compare with (
5.6
), (
5.9
), and (
5.13
) in step one). By (
5.38
) we find

-{\rm res}_{W}(F^{\prime},\nu)=\lim_{r\to\infty}P(F;\textbf{{C}}_{r}^{\nu}\setminus W)-P(G_{r};\textbf{{C}}_{r,(\alpha,\beta)}^{\nu})\,.

(5.39)

In order to relate the residue of
(F^{\prime},\nu)
to
\psi_{W}(v_{j})-P(B^{(v_{j})})
we consider the sets

Z_{j}=(G_{r}\cap\textbf{{C}}_{r,(\alpha,b)}^{\nu})\cup(E_{j}\setminus\textbf{{C}}_{r,(\alpha,\beta)}^{\nu})
, which, by isoperimetry, satisfy

\displaystyle P(Z_{j})\!\geq\!P(B^{(|E_{j}\setminus\textbf{{C}}_{r,(\alpha,\beta)}^{\nu}|)})\geq P(B^{(v_{j})})-C(n)\,r^{n}\,(\beta-\alpha)\,\lambda_{j}^{-1}\,.

(5.40)

Since for a.e.
r>R^{\prime}
we have

P(Z_{j})=P(E_{j};\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\!\setminus\!\textbf{{C}}_{r,(\alpha,\beta)}^{\nu})+P(G_{r};\textbf{{C}}_{r,(\alpha,b)}^{\nu})+\mathcal{H}^{n}\big((E_{j}\Delta G_{r})\cap\partial\textbf{{C}}_{r,(\alpha,b)}^{\nu}\big)\,,

we conclude that

\displaystyle\psi_{W}(v_{j})-P(B^{(v_{j})})\!\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!P(E_{j};\textbf{{C}}_{r,(\alpha,\beta)}^{\nu}\!\setminus\!W)\!+P(E_{j};\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\!\setminus\!\textbf{{C}}_{r,(\alpha,\beta)}^{\nu})\!-\!P(B^{(v_{j})})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!P(E_{j};\textbf{{C}}_{r,(\alpha,\beta)}^{\nu}\setminus W)+P(Z_{j})-P(B^{(v_{j})})

\displaystyle\!\!\!-P(G_{r};\textbf{{C}}_{r,(\alpha,b)}^{\nu})-\mathcal{H}^{n}\big((E_{j}\Delta G_{r})\cap\partial\textbf{{C}}_{r,(\alpha,b)}^{\nu}\big)

so that
E_{j}\to F
in
L^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})
and (
5.40
) give, for a.e.
r>R^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\varliminf_{j\to\infty}\psi_{W}(v_{j})-P(B^{(v_{j})})\geq P(F;\textbf{{C}}_{r,(\alpha,\beta)}^{\nu}\!\setminus\!W)-P(G_{r};\textbf{{C}}_{r,(\alpha,b)}^{\nu})

\displaystyle-\mathcal{H}^{n}\big((F\Delta G_{r})\cap\partial\textbf{{C}}_{r,(\alpha,\beta)}^{\nu}\big)\geq P(F;\textbf{{C}}_{r}^{\nu}\!\setminus\!W)-P(G_{r};\textbf{{C}}_{r}^{\nu})-C(n,W)/r\,,

thanks to (
5.37
) and
(F\Delta G_{r})\cap\partial\textbf{{C}}_{r}^{\nu}=(F\Delta G_{r})\cap\partial\textbf{{C}}_{r,(\alpha,\beta)}^{\nu}
. Letting
r\to\infty
, recalling (
5.39
), and by
(F^{\prime},\nu)\in\mathcal{F}
, we find
\varliminf_{j\to\infty}\psi_{W}(v_{j})-P(B^{(v_{j})})\geq-{\rm res}_{W}(F^{\prime},\nu)\geq-\mathcal{R}(W)
. This completes the proof of (
5.35
), which in turn, combined with (
5.1
), gives (
1.19
), and also shows that
L^{1}_{\rm loc}
-subsequential limits
F
of
E_{j}\in{\rm Min}[\psi_{W}(v_{j})]
for
v_{j}\to\infty
are such that, for some
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{n}
,
(F\cup W,\nu)\in\mathcal{F}
and
F^{\prime}=F\cup W\in{\rm Max}[\mathcal{R}(W)]
.

Step four:
Moving towards the proof of (
1.22
), we prove the validity, uniformly among varifolds associated to maximizers of
\mathcal{R}(W)
, of estimates analogous to (
5.29
) and (
5.30
).
For a constant
\Gamma>2\,n\,\omega_{n}
to be determined later on (see (
5.52
), (
5.53
), and (
5.54
) below) in dependence of
n
and
W
, and for
\sigma>0
, we let
M_{0}=M_{0}(n,2\,\Gamma,\sigma)
and
\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon_{0}(n,2\,\Gamma,\sigma)
be determined by Theorem
2.1
. If
(F,\nu)\in{\rm Max}[\mathcal{R}(W)]
, then by Theorem
1.1
-(ii) we can find
R_{2}=R_{2}(W)>0
,
f\in C^{\infty}(\nu^{\perp})
such that

(\partial F)\setminus\textbf{{C}}_{R_{2}}^{\nu}=\big\{x+f(x)\,\nu:x\in\nu^{\perp}\,,|x|>R_{2}\big\}\,,

(5.41)

and such that (
1.14
) holds with
\max\{|a|,|b|,|c|\}\leq C(W)
and
|\nabla f(x)|\leq C_{0}/|x|^{n-1}
for
|x|>R_{2}
. Thus
\|\nabla f\|_{C^{0}(\nu^{\perp}\setminus\textbf{{D}}_{r}^{\nu})}\to 0
as
r\to\infty
uniformly on
(F,\nu)\in{\rm Max}[\mathcal{R}(W)]
, and there is
R_{3}>\max\{2\,R_{2},1\}
(depending on
W
) such that, if

V_{F}=\mathbf{var}\,((\partial F)\setminus B_{R_{3}},1)
, then
V_{F}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}(0,R_{3},\infty)
, and

\|{\rm bd}_{V_{F}}\|(\partial B_{R_{3}})\leq\Gamma\,R_{3}^{n-1}\,,\qquad\|V_{F}\|(B_{\rho}\setminus B_{R_{3}})\leq\Gamma\,\rho^{n}\qquad\forall\rho>R_{3}\,,

(5.42)

(compare with (
5.29
)). Then, arguing as in step three-(c), or more simply by exploiting (
5.41
) and the decay estimates (
1.14
), we see that there is
L>\max\{M_{0},64\}
, depending on
n
,
W
and
\sigma
only, such that, setting

s_{W}(\sigma)=2\,L\,R_{3}

(5.43)

we have for some
c(n)>0
(compare with (
5.30
))

\displaystyle|\delta_{V_{F},R_{3},0}(s_{W}(\sigma)/8)|\leq\varepsilon_{0}/2\,,\qquad\inf_{r>s_{W}(\sigma)/8}\delta_{V_{F},R_{3},0}(r)\geq-\varepsilon_{0}/2\,.

(5.44)

Step five:
Given
E_{j}\in{\rm Min}[\psi_{W}(v_{j})]
for
v_{j}\to\infty
, we prove the existence of
(F,\nu)\in{\rm Max}[\mathcal{R}(W)]
and
h_{j}\in C^{\infty}((\partial F)\setminus B_{R_{2}})
such that

\displaystyle(\partial E_{j})\cap A^{R_{1}\,\lambda_{j}}_{4\,R_{2}}=\Big\{y+h_{j}(y)\,\nu_{F}(y):y\in\partial F\Big\}\cap A^{R_{1}\,\lambda_{j}}_{4\,R_{2}}\,,

(5.45)

\displaystyle\lim_{j\to\infty}\|h_{j}\|_{C^{1}((\partial F)\cap A_{4\,R_{2}}^{M})}=0\,,\qquad\forall M<\infty\,;

(5.46)

and that if
x_{j}
satisfies
|E_{j}\Delta B^{(v_{j})}(x_{j})|=\inf_{x}|E_{j}\Delta B^{(v_{j})}(x)|
, then

\lim_{j\to\infty}||x_{j}|^{-1}\,x_{j}-\nu|=0\,;

(5.47)

finally, we prove statement (iii) (i.e.,
(\partial E_{j})\setminus B_{R_{2}}
is diffeomorphic to an
n
-dimensional disk). By step three, there is
(F,\nu)\in{\rm Max}[\mathcal{R}(W)]
such that, up to extracting subsequences, (
5.18
) holds. By (
5.18
) and (
5.41
), and with
s_{W}(\sigma)
defined as in step four (see (
5.43
)) starting from
F
, we can apply Remark
3.2
to find
f_{j}\in C^{\infty}(\nu^{\perp})
such that

(\partial E_{j})\cap A^{s_{W}(\sigma)}_{2\,R_{2}}=\big\{x+f_{j}(x)\,\nu:x\in\nu^{\perp}\big\}\cap A^{s_{W}(\sigma)}_{2\,R_{2}}\,,

(5.48)

for
j
large enough (in terms of
\sigma
,
n
,
W
, and
F
), and such that
f_{j}\to f
in
C^{1}(\textbf{{D}}_{s_{W}(\sigma)}^{\nu}\setminus\textbf{{D}}_{2\,R_{2}}^{\nu})
.
With
R_{3}
as in step four and with the goal of applying Theorem
2.1
to the varifolds

V_{j}=\mathbf{var}\,((\partial E_{j})\setminus B_{R_{3}},1)
, we notice that
V_{j}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}(\Lambda_{j},R_{3},\infty)
, for some
\Lambda_{j}\leq\Lambda_{0}\,\lambda_{j}^{-1}
(thanks to (
3.1
)). In particular, by (
5.43
),
s_{W}(\sigma)
satisfies the “mesoscale bounds” (compare with (
2.4
))

\varepsilon_{0}\,(4\,\Lambda_{j})^{-1}>s_{W}(\sigma)>\max\{M_{0},64\}\,R_{3}

(5.49)

provided
j
is large. Moreover, by
R_{3}>2\,R_{2}
and
s_{W}(\sigma)/8>2\,R_{2}
, by (
5.41
), (
5.48
) and
f_{j}\to f
in
C^{1}
, we exploit (
5.42
) and (
5.44
) to deduce

\displaystyle\|{\rm bd}_{V_{j}}\|(\partial B_{R_{3}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(2\,\Gamma)\,R_{3}^{n-1}\,,

(5.50)

\displaystyle|\delta_{V_{j},R_{3},0}(s_{W}(\sigma)/8)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(2/3)\,\varepsilon_{0}\,.

(5.51)

We claim that, up to increasing
\Gamma
(depending on
n
and
W
), we can entail

\|V_{j}\|(B_{\rho}\setminus B_{R_{3}})\leq\Gamma\,\rho^{n}\,,\qquad\forall\rho>R_{3}\,.

(5.52)

Indeed, by Theorem
3.1
-(i), for some positive
\Lambda_{0}
and
s_{0}
depending on
W
only,
E_{j}
is a
(\Lambda_{0}\,\lambda_{j}^{-1},s_{0}\,\lambda_{j})
-perimeter minimizer with free boundary in
\Omega
. Comparing
E_{j}
to
E_{j}\setminus B_{r}
by (
3.1
), for every
r<s_{0}\,\lambda_{j}
,

P(E_{j};\Omega\cap B_{r})\leq C(n)\,\big(r^{n}+\Lambda_{0}\,\lambda_{j}^{-1}\,r^{n+1}\big)\leq C(n,W)\,r^{n}\,;

(5.53)

since, at the same time, if
r>s_{0}\,\lambda_{j}
, then

P(E_{j};\Omega\cap B_{r})\leq P(E_{j};\Omega)=\psi_{W}(v_{j})\leq P(B^{(v_{j})})\leq C(n)\,s_{0}^{-n}\,r^{n}\,,

(5.54)

by combining (
5.53
) and (
5.54
) we find (
5.52
). With (
5.50
) and (
5.52
) at hand, we can also show that

|\delta_{V_{j},R_{3},\Lambda_{j}}(s_{W}(\sigma)/8)|\leq\varepsilon_{0}\,.

(5.55)

Indeed, by
s_{W}(\sigma)=2\,L\,R_{3}
and by
\Lambda_{j}\leq\Lambda_{0}\,\lambda_{j}^{-1}
,

\displaystyle\big|\delta_{V_{j},R_{3},\Lambda_{j}}(s_{W}(\sigma)/8)-\delta_{V_{j},R_{3},0}(s_{W}(\sigma)/8)\big|

\displaystyle\leq(\Lambda_{0}/\lambda_{j})\,\int_{R_{3}}^{s_{W}(\sigma)/8}\!\!\!\!\rho^{-n}\,\|V_{j}\|(B_{\rho}\setminus B_{R_{3}})\,d\rho\leq\frac{\Lambda_{0}\,R_{3}\,\Gamma}{\lambda_{j}}\,\Big(\frac{L}{4}-1\Big)\leq\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{3}\,,

provided
j
is large enough. To complete checking that Theorem
2.1
can be applied to every
V_{j}
with
j
large enough, we now consider the quantities

R_{*j}=\sup\big\{\rho>s_{W}(\sigma)/8:\delta_{V_{j},R_{3},\Lambda_{j}}(\rho)\geq-\varepsilon_{0}\big\}\,,

and prove that, for a constant
\tau_{0}
depending on
n
and
W
only, we have

R_{*j}\geq\tau_{0}\,\lambda_{j}\,;

(5.56)

in particular, provided
j
is large enough, (
5.56
) implies immediately

R_{*j}\geq 4\,s_{W}(\sigma)\,,

(5.57)

which was the last assumption in Theorem
2.1
that needed to be checked. To prove (
5.56
), we pick
\tau_{0}
such that

\big|\tau_{0}^{-n}\,\mathcal{H}^{n}\big(B_{\tau_{0}}(z)\cap\partial B^{(1)}\big)-\omega_{n}\big|\leq\varepsilon_{0}/2\,,\qquad\forall z\in\partial B^{(1)}\,.

(5.58)

(Of course this condition only requires
\tau_{0}
to depend on
n
.) By definition of
x_{j}
and by (
3.4
), and up to extracting a subsequence, we have
x_{j}\to z_{0}
for some
z_{0}\in\partial B^{(1)}
. In particular, setting
\rho_{j}=\tau_{0}\,\lambda_{j}
, we find

\displaystyle\rho_{j}^{-n}\,\|V_{j}\|(B_{\rho_{j}}\setminus B_{R_{3}})\!\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!\tau_{0}^{-n}\,P\big((E_{j}-x_{j})/\lambda_{j}\,;\,B_{\tau_{0}}(-x_{j})\setminus B_{R_{3}/\rho_{j}}(-x_{j})\big)

\displaystyle\to\tau_{0}^{-n}\,\mathcal{H}^{n}\big(B_{\tau_{0}}(-z_{0})\cap\partial B^{(1)}\big)\leq\omega_{n}+(\varepsilon_{0}/2)\,,

thus proving that, for
j
large enough,

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\delta_{V_{j},R_{3},\Lambda_{j}}(\rho_{j})\!\!\geq\!\!-\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{2}+\frac{1}{n\,\rho_{j}^{n}}\,\int\,x\cdot\nu^{\rm co}_{V_{j}}\,d\,{\rm bd}_{V_{j}}-\Lambda_{j}\,\int_{R_{3}}^{\rho_{j}}\frac{\|V_{j}\|(B_{\rho}\setminus B_{R_{3}})}{\rho^{n}}\,d\rho

\displaystyle\geq\!\!-\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{2}-\frac{2\,\Gamma\,R_{3}^{n}}{n\,\tau_{0}^{n}\,\lambda_{j}}-\Lambda_{0}\,\Gamma\,\frac{(\rho_{j}-R_{3})}{\lambda_{j}}\geq-\frac{\varepsilon_{0}}{2}-\frac{C_{*}(n,W)}{\tau_{0}^{n}\,\lambda_{j}}-C_{**}(n,W)\,\tau_{0}\,,

where we have used (
5.50
),
{\rm spt}\,{\rm bd}_{V_{j}}\subset\partial B_{R_{3}}
, and (
5.52
). Therefore, provided we pick
\tau_{0}
depending on
n
and
W
so that
C_{**}\,\tau_{0}\leq\varepsilon_{0}/4
, and then we pick
j
large enough to entail
(C_{*}(n,W)/\tau_{0}^{n})\lambda_{j}^{-1}\leq\varepsilon_{0}/4
, we conclude that if
r\in(R_{3},\rho_{j}]
, then

\delta_{V_{j},R_{3},\Lambda_{j}}(r)\geq\delta_{V_{j},R_{3},\Lambda_{j}}(\rho_{j})\geq-\varepsilon_{0}
,
where in the first inequality we have used Theorem
2.7
-(i) and the fact that
V_{j}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}(\Lambda_{j},R_{3},\infty)
. In summary, by (
5.50
) and (
5.52
) (which give (
2.3
)), by (
5.49
) (which gives (
2.4
) with
s=s_{W}(\sigma)/8
), and by (
5.55
) and (
5.57
) (which imply, respectively, (
2.5
) and (
2.6
)) we see that Theorem
2.1
-(i) can be applied with
V=V_{j}
and
s=s_{W}(\sigma)/8
provided
j
is large in terms of
\sigma
,
n
,
W
and the limit
F
of the
E_{j}
’s. Thus, setting

S_{*j}=\min\big\{R_{*j},\varepsilon_{0}/\Lambda_{j}\big\}\,,

(5.59)

and noticing that by (
5.56
) and
\Lambda_{j}\leq\Lambda_{0}\,\lambda_{j}^{-1}
we have

S_{*j}\geq 16\,R_{1}\,\lambda_{j}\,,

(5.60)

(for
R_{1}
depending on
n
and
W
only) we conclude that, for
j
large, there are
K_{j}\in\mathcal{H}
and
u_{j}\in\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}(\Sigma_{K_{j}},\sigma_{W}(\sigma)/32,R_{1}\,\lambda_{j})
, such that

(\partial E_{j})\cap A_{s_{W}(\sigma)/32}^{R_{1}\,\lambda_{j}}=\Sigma_{K_{j}}\big(u_{j},s_{W}(\sigma)/32,R_{1}\,\lambda_{j}\big)\,.

(5.61)

Similarly, by (
5.42
) and (
5.44
), thanks to Theorem
2.1
-(ii) we have

(\partial F)\cap\big(\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\setminus B_{s_{W}(\sigma)/32}\big)=\Sigma_{\nu^{\perp}}\big(u,s_{W}(\sigma)/32,\infty\big)\,,

(5.62)

for
u\in\mathcal{X}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(\Sigma_{\nu^{\perp}},s_{W}(\sigma)/32,\infty)
for every
\sigma^{\prime}>\sigma
. Now, by
E_{j}\to F
in
L^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})
, (
5.61
) and (
5.62
) can hold only if
|\nu_{K_{j}}-\nu|\leq\zeta(\sigma)
for a function
\zeta
, depending on
n
and
W
only, such that
\zeta(\sigma)\to 0
as
\sigma\to 0^{+}
. In particular (denoting by
\sigma_{0}^{*}
,
\varepsilon_{0}^{*}
and
