the dimension dependent constants originally introduced in Lemma
2.5
as
\sigma_{0}
,
\varepsilon_{0}
and
C_{0}
) we can find
\sigma_{1}=\sigma_{1}(n,W)\leq\sigma_{0}^{*}
such that if
\sigma<\sigma_{1}
, then
\varepsilon_{0}^{*}\geq\zeta(\sigma)\geq|\nu_{K_{j}}-\nu|
, and correspondingly, Lemma
2.5
-(i) can be used to infer the existence of

u_{j}^{*}\in\mathcal{X}_{C_{0}\,(\sigma+\zeta(\sigma))}(\Sigma_{\nu^{\perp}},s_{W}(\sigma)/32,2\,R_{1}\,\lambda_{j})
such that, for
j
large,

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\nu^{\perp}}\big(u_{j}^{*},s_{W}(\sigma)/32,2\,R_{1}\,\lambda_{j}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Sigma_{K_{j}}\big(u_{j},s_{W}(\sigma)/32,2\,R_{1}\,\lambda_{j}\big)

(5.63)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\partial E_{j})\cap A_{s_{W}(\sigma)/32}^{2\,R_{1}\,\lambda_{j}}\,.

By (
5.48
) and Lemma
B.1
, (
5.63
) implies cylindrical graphicality: more precisely, provided
\sigma_{1}
is small enough, there are
g_{j}\in C^{1}(\nu^{\perp})
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\nu^{\perp}}\{|g_{j}(x)|\,|x|^{-1},|\nabla g_{j}(x)|\}\leq C\,\big(\sigma+\zeta(\sigma)\big)\,,

(5.64)

\displaystyle(\partial E_{j})\cap A^{R_{1}\,\lambda_{j}}_{2\,R_{2}}=\big\{x+g_{j}(x)\,\nu:x\in\nu^{\perp}\big\}\cap A^{R_{1}\,\lambda_{j}}_{2\,R_{2}}\,.

(5.65)

At the same time, by (
5.41
), (
1.14
), and up to further increasing
R_{2}
and decreasing
\sigma_{1}
, we can exploit Lemma
B.2
in the appendix to find
h_{j}\in C^{1}(G(f))
,
G(f)=\{x+f(x)\,\nu:x\in\nu^{\perp}\}
, such that

\big\{x+g_{j}(x)\,\nu:x\in\nu^{\perp}\big\}\setminus B_{4\,R_{2}}=\big\{z+h_{j}(z)\,\nu_{F}(z):z\in G(f)\big\}\setminus B_{4\,R_{2}}

which, combined with (
5.41
) and (
5.65
) shows that

(\partial E_{j})\cap A^{R_{1}\,\lambda_{j}}_{4\,R_{2}}=\big\{z+h_{j}(z)\,\nu_{F}(z):z\in\partial F\big\}\cap A^{R_{1}\,\lambda_{j}}_{4\,R_{2}}

that is (
5.45
). By
E_{j}\to F
in
L^{1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})
, we find
h_{j}\to 0
in
L^{1}((\partial F)\cap A_{4\,R_{2}}^{M})
for every
M<\infty
, so that, by elliptic regularity, (
5.46
) follows. We now recall that, by Theorem
3.1
-(ii),
(\partial E_{j})\setminus B_{R_{0}(v_{j})\,\lambda_{j}}
coincides with

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\big\{y+\lambda_{j}w_{j}\big((y-x_{j})/\lambda_{j}\big)\,\nu_{B^{(v_{j})}(x_{j})}(y):y\in\partial B^{(v_{j})}(x_{j})\big\}\setminus B_{R_{0}(v_{j})\,\lambda_{j}}

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\mbox{for $\|w_{j}\|_{C^{1}(\partial B^{(1)})}\to 0$ and $R_{0}(v_{j})\to 0$}\,.

(5.66)

The overlapping of (
5.65
) and (
5.66
) (i.e., the fact that
R_{0}(v_{j})<R_{1}
if
j
is large enough) implies statement (iii). Finally, combining (
5.64
) and (
5.65
) with (
5.66
) and
\|w_{j}\|_{C^{1}(\partial B^{(1)})}\to 0
we deduce the validity of (
5.47
). More precisely, rescaling by
\lambda_{j}
in (
5.64
) and (
5.65
) and setting
E_{j}^{*}=E_{j}/\lambda_{j}
, we find
g_{j}^{*}\in C^{1}(\nu^{\perp})
such that, for every
j\geq j_{0}(\sigma)
and
\sigma<\sigma_{1}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\nu^{\perp}}\{|g_{j}^{*}(x)||x|^{-1},|\nabla g_{j}^{*}(x)|\}\leq C\,\big(\sigma+\zeta(\sigma)\big)\,,

(5.67)

\displaystyle(\partial E_{j}^{*})\cap A^{R_{1}}_{2\,R_{2}/\lambda_{j}}=\big\{x+g_{j}^{*}(x)\,\nu:x\in\nu^{\perp}\big\}\cap A^{R_{1}}_{2\,R_{2}/\lambda_{j}}\,,

(5.68)

while rescaling by
\lambda_{j}
in (
5.66
) and setting
z_{j}=x_{j}/\lambda_{j}
we find

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!(\partial E_{j}^{*})\setminus B_{R_{0}(v_{j})}\!\!=\!\big\{z_{j}+z+w_{j}(z)\,\nu_{B^{(1)}}(z)::y\in\partial B^{(1)}(z_{j})\big\}\!\setminus\!B_{R_{0}(v_{j})}

(5.69)

where
||z_{j}|-\omega_{n+1}^{1/(n+1)}|\to 0
thanks to (
3.4
). Up to subsequences,
z_{j}\to z_{0}
, where
|z_{0}|=\omega_{n+1}^{1/(n+1)}
. Should
z_{0}\neq|z_{0}|\,\nu
, then picking
\sigma
small enough in terms of
|\nu-(z_{0}/|z_{0}|)|>0
and picking
j
large enough, we would then be able to exploit (
5.67
) to get a contradiction with
\|w_{j}\|_{C^{1}(\partial B^{(1)})}\to 0
.

Conclusion
: Theorem
3.1
implies Theorem
1.6
-(i), and (
1.19
) was proved in step three. Should Theorem
1.6
-(ii), (iii), or (iv) fail, then we could find a sequence
\{(E_{j},v_{j})\}_{j}
contradicting the conclusions of either step five or Theorem
3.1
. We have thus completed the proof of Theorem
1.6
.
∎

Appendix A
Proof of Theorem
2.6

We assume
H\in\mathcal{H}
,
\Lambda\geq 0
,
\eta_{0}>\eta>0
,
(r_{1},r_{2})
and
(r_{3},r_{4})
are
(\eta,\eta_{0})
-related as in (
2.41
), and
u\in\mathcal{X}_{\sigma}(\Sigma_{H},r_{1},r_{2})
is such that
\Sigma_{H}(u,r_{1},r_{2})
has mean curvature bounded by
\Lambda
in
A_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}
. We want to find
\sigma_{0}
and
C_{0}
, depending on
n
,
\eta_{0}
, and
\eta
only, such that, if
\max\{1,\Lambda\,r_{2}\}\,\sigma\leq\sigma_{0}
, then

\Big|\mathcal{H}^{n}(\Sigma_{H}(u,r_{3},r_{4}))-\mathcal{H}^{n}(\Sigma_{H}(0,r_{3},r_{4}))\Big|\leq C_{0}\,\int_{\Sigma_{H}\times(r_{1},r_{2})}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!r^{n-1}\,\big(u^{2}+\Lambda\,r\,|u|\big);

(A.1)

and such that, if there is
r\in(r_{1},r_{2})
s.t.
E_{\Sigma_{H}}^{0}[u_{r}]=0
on
\Sigma_{H}
, then

\int_{\Sigma_{H}\times(r_{3},r_{4})}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!r^{n-1}\,u^{2}\leq C(n)\,\Lambda\,r_{2}\,(r_{2}^{n}-r_{1}^{n})+C_{0}\,\int_{\Sigma_{H}\times(r_{1},r_{2})}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!r^{n-1}\,(r\,\partial_{r}u)^{2}\,.

(A.2)

We make three preliminary considerations:
(i):
By
[
AA81
, 4.5(8)]

\displaystyle\Big|\mathcal{H}^{n}(\Sigma_{H}(u,r_{1},r_{2}))-\mathcal{H}^{n}(\Sigma_{H}(0,r_{1},r_{2}))

(A.3)

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma_{H}\times(r_{1},r_{2})}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!r^{n-1}\,\big(|\nabla^{\Sigma_{H}}u|^{2}+(r\,\partial_{r}u)^{2}-(n-1)\,u^{2}\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\leq C(n)\,\sigma\,\int_{\Sigma_{H}\times(r_{1},r_{2})}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!r^{n-1}\big(u^{2}+|\nabla^{\Sigma_{H}}u|^{2}+(r\,\partial_{r}u)^{2}\big)\,.

Similarly, by the last displayed formula on
[
AA81
, Page 236]
and by
[
AA81
, Lemma 4.9(1)]
, if
\varphi=\psi^{2}\,w
,
w\in C^{1}(\Sigma_{H}\times(r_{1},r_{2}))
and
\psi\in C^{1}(r_{1},r_{2})
, then

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{d}{dt}\bigg|_{t=0}\!\!\!\!\!\mathcal{H}^{n}(\Sigma_{H}(u+t\,\varphi,r_{1},r_{2}))

(A.4)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\int_{\Sigma_{H}\times(r_{1},r_{2})}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!r^{n-1}\,\Big\{\nabla^{\Sigma_{H}}u\cdot\nabla^{\Sigma_{H}}\varphi+(r\,\partial_{r}u)\,(r\,\partial_{r}\varphi)-(n-1)\,u\,\varphi\Big\}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(n)\,\sigma\,\int_{\Sigma_{H}\times(r_{1},r_{2})}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!r^{n-1}\,\psi^{2}\,\big\{|\nabla^{\Sigma_{H}}u|^{2}+|\nabla^{\Sigma_{H}}w|^{2}+(r\,\partial_{r}u)^{2}+(r\,\partial_{r}w)^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 156.49014pt+\,u^{2}+w^{2}+(r\,\psi^{\prime})^{2}\,w^{2}\big\}\,,

which is the second order expansion of the first variation of the area at
\Sigma_{H}(u,r_{1},r_{2})
along outer variations in spherical coordinates of the form
\varphi=\psi^{2}\,w
,
\psi=\psi(r)
.
(ii):
For the sake of brevity, given
\zeta:(r_{1},r_{2})\to\mathbb{R}
a radial function,
u,v:\Sigma_{H}\times(r_{1},r_{2})\to\mathbb{R}
,
X,Y:\Sigma_{H}\times(r_{1},r_{2})\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
, we set

\displaystyle Q_{\zeta}(u,v)=\int_{\Sigma_{H}\times(r_{1},r_{2})}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!r^{n-1}\,\zeta(r)^{2}\,u\,v\,,\quad Q_{\zeta}(X,Y)=\int_{\Sigma_{H}\times(r_{1},r_{2})}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!r^{n-1}\,\zeta(r)^{2}\,X\cdot Y\,,

and
Q_{\zeta}(u)=Q_{\zeta}(u,u)
,
Q_{\zeta}(X)=Q_{\zeta}(X,X)
.
(iii):
The following two estimates (whose elementary proof is contained in
[
AA81
, Lemma 7.13]
) hold: whenever
v\in C^{1}(\Sigma_{H}\times(r_{1},r_{2}))
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{H}\times(r_{1},r_{2})}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!r^{n-1}\,v^{2}\leq C(n,\eta,\eta_{0})\,\Big\{\int_{\Sigma_{H}\times(r_{1},r_{2})}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!r^{n-1}\,(r\,\partial_{r}v)^{2}+\int_{\Sigma_{H}\times(r_{3},r_{4})}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!r^{n-1}\,v^{2}\Big\},

(A.5)

and, provided there is
r\in[r_{1},r_{2}]
such that
v_{r}=0
on
\Sigma_{H}
, we have

\int_{\Sigma_{H}\times(r_{1},r_{2})}r^{n-1}\,v^{2}\leq C(n,\eta_{0})\,\int_{\Sigma_{H}\times(r_{1},r_{2})}r^{n-1}\,(r\,\partial_{r}v)^{2}\,.

(A.6)

We are now ready for the proof. Compared to
[
AA81
, Chapter 4]
, the main difference is that we replace
[
AA81
, Lemma 4.10]
with (
A.7
).

Step one:
We prove that there is
h:\Sigma_{H}\times(r_{1},r_{2})\to[-\Lambda,\Lambda]
such that for every
w\in C^{1}(\Sigma_{H}\times(r_{1},r_{2}))
and
\psi\in C^{1}(r_{1},r_{2})
we have

\displaystyle\Big|T_{\psi}(u,w)-\int_{\Sigma_{H}\times(r_{1},r_{2})}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!r^{n}\,\psi^{2}\,w\,h\Big|\leq C(n)\sigma_{0}\big(Q_{\psi}(u)+Q_{\psi}(w)+Q_{\psi}(\nabla^{\Sigma_{H}}u)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+Q_{\psi}(\nabla^{\Sigma_{H}}w)+Q_{r\,\psi}(\partial_{r}u)+Q_{r\,\psi}(\partial_{r}w)+Q_{r\,\psi^{\prime}}(w)\big)\,.

(A.7)

where
T_{\psi}(u,w)=Q_{\psi}(\nabla^{\Sigma_{H}}u,\nabla^{\Sigma_{H}}w)+Q_{r}\big(\partial_{r}u,\partial_{r}[\psi^{2}w]\big)-(n-1)\,Q_{\psi}(u,w)
.
We start rewriting (
A.4
) as

\displaystyle\Big|T_{\psi}(u,w)-\frac{d}{dt}\Big|_{t=0}\,\mathcal{H}^{n}(\Sigma(u+t\,\psi^{2}\,w,r_{1},r_{2}))\Big|

(A.8)

\displaystyle\leq C(n)\,\sigma\,\big(Q_{\psi}(u)+Q_{\psi}(w)+Q_{\psi}(\nabla^{\Sigma_{H}}u)+Q_{\psi}(\nabla^{\Sigma_{H}}w)

\displaystyle\hskip 156.49014pt+Q_{r\,\psi}(\partial_{r}u)+Q_{r\,\psi}(\partial_{r}w)+Q_{r\,\psi^{\prime}}(w)\big)\,.

If
F_{u+t\,\varphi}:{\Sigma_{H}}\times(r_{1},r_{2})\to{\Sigma_{H}}(u+t\,\varphi,r_{1},r_{2})
,
\varphi=\psi^{2}\,w
, is given by

F_{u+t\,\varphi}(\omega,r)=r\,\frac{\omega+(u(\omega,r)+t\varphi(\omega,r))\,\nu_{H}}{\sqrt{1+(u(\omega,r)+t\varphi(\omega,r))^{2}}}\,,

then
\{\Phi_{t}=F_{u+t\,\varphi}\circ(F_{u})^{-1}\}_{t\in[0,1]}
are diffeomorphisms on
{\Sigma_{H}}(u,r_{1},r_{2})
, with
\Phi_{t}(\Sigma_{H}(u,r_{1},r_{2}))=\Sigma_{H}(u+t\,\varphi,r_{1},r_{2})
and
\dot{\Phi}_{0}=(d/dt)_{t=0}\Phi_{t}
. Since
\Sigma_{H}(u,r_{1},r_{2})
has mean curvature bounded by
\Lambda
in
A_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}
, for some bounded function
h:\Sigma_{H}\times(r_{1},r_{2})\to[-\Lambda,\Lambda]
we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\Big|_{t=0}\,\mathcal{H}^{n}(\Sigma(u+t\varphi,r_{1},r_{2}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Lambda\,\int_{\Sigma_{H}(u,r_{1},r_{2})}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!h(F_{u}^{-1})\,\dot{\Phi}_{0}\cdot\nu_{\Sigma_{H}(u,r_{1},r_{2})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Lambda\,\int_{\Sigma_{H}\times(r_{1},r_{2})}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!h\,\dot{\Phi}_{0}\big(F_{u}\big)\cdot\star\big(\partial_{r}F_{u}\wedge\,\wedge_{i=1}^{n-1}\partial_{i}F_{u}\big)\,,

where
\partial_{i}=\nabla_{\tau_{i}}
for a local orthonormal frame
\{\tau_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n-1}
in
\Sigma_{H}
, and where
\star
is the Hodge star-operator (so that
\star\,(v_{1}\wedge v_{2}...\wedge v_{n})
is a normal vector to the hyperplane spanned by the
v_{i}
s, with length equal to the
n
-dimensional volume of the parallelogram defined by the
v_{i}
s, and whose orientation depends on the ordering of the
v_{i}
s themselves). We can compute the initial velocity
\dot{\Phi}_{0}
of
\{\Phi_{t}\}_{t\in[0,1]}
by noticing that

\Phi_{t}\big(F_{u}(\omega,r)\big)=r\,(1+(u+t\,\varphi)^{2})^{-1/2}\,(\omega+(u+t\,\varphi)\,\nu_{H})
, so that,

\displaystyle\dot{\Phi}_{0}(F_{u})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\Big|_{t=0}\,r\,\frac{\omega+(u+t\,\varphi)\,\nu_{H}}{\sqrt{1+(u+t\,\varphi)^{2}}}=r\,\frac{-u\,\varphi\,\omega+\varphi\,\nu_{H}}{(1+u^{2})^{3/2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r\,\big(-u\,\varphi\,\omega+\varphi\,\nu_{H})+r\,\sigma\,{\rm O}\big(\psi^{2}\,(u^{2}+w^{2})\big)\,.

At the same time

\displaystyle\partial_{r}F_{u}\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!\frac{\omega+u\,\nu_{H}}{\sqrt{1+u^{2}}}\!+r\,\partial_{r}\Big(\frac{\omega+u\,\nu_{H}}{\sqrt{1+u^{2}}}\Big)\!\!=\!\frac{\omega+u\,\nu_{H}}{\sqrt{1+u^{2}}}\!-\frac{r\,u\,\partial_{r}u\,\omega}{(1+u^{2})^{3/2}}+\!\frac{r\,\partial_{r}u\,\nu_{H}}{(1+u^{2})^{3/2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!\big(1-(u^{2}/2)-u\,r\,\partial_{r}u\big)\,\omega+\big(u+r\,\partial_{r}u\big)\,\nu_{H}+\sigma\,{\rm O}(u^{2}+(r\partial_{r}u)^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!A\,\omega+B\,\nu_{H}+\sigma\,{\rm O}(u^{2}+(r\partial_{r}u)^{2})\,,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{i}F_{u}}{r}\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!\partial_{i}\Big(\frac{\omega+u\,\nu_{H}}{\sqrt{1+u^{2}}}\Big)=\frac{\tau_{i}}{\sqrt{1+u^{2}}}-\frac{u\,\partial_{i}u}{(1+u^{2})^{3/2}}\,\omega+\frac{\partial_{i}u}{(1+u^{2})^{3/2}}\,\nu_{H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(1-(u^{2}/2)\big)\,\tau_{i}-u\,\partial_{i}u\,\omega+\partial_{i}u\,\nu_{H}+\sigma\,{\rm O}(u^{2}+(\partial_{i}u)^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C\,\tau_{i}+E_{i}\,\omega+F_{i}\,\nu_{H}+\sigma\,{\rm O}(u^{2}+(\partial_{i}u)^{2})

so that, with
\Xi=\wedge_{i=1}^{n-1}\tau_{i}
,
\hat{\tau}_{i}=\wedge_{j\neq i}\tau_{j}
, and
P(u)^{2}=u^{2}+|\nabla^{\Sigma_{H}}u|^{2}+(r\partial_{r}u)^{2}
,

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\frac{\partial_{r}F_{u}\wedge\wedge_{i=1}^{n-1}\partial_{i}F_{u}}{r^{n-1}}\!=\!\big(A\,\omega+B\,\nu_{H}\big)\!\wedge\!\wedge_{i=1}^{n-1}\big(C\,\tau_{i}+E_{i}\,\omega+F_{i}\,\nu_{H}\big)\!+\!\sigma{\rm O}\big(P(u)^{2}\big)

\displaystyle=A\,C^{n-1}\,\omega\wedge\Xi+B\,C^{n-1}\,\nu_{H}\wedge\Xi\,+G_{i}\,\big(\omega\wedge\nu_{H}\wedge\hat{\tau}_{i}\big)+\sigma{\rm O}\big(P(u)^{2}\big),

for a coefficient
G_{i}
which we do not need to compute. Indeed,
\star(\omega\wedge\nu_{H}\wedge\hat{\tau}_{i})
, being parallel to
\tau_{i}
, is orthogonal to
\omega
and
\nu_{H}
, so that

\displaystyle r^{-n}\,\dot{\Phi}_{0}\big(F_{u}(r,\omega)\big)\cdot\star\big(\partial_{r}F_{u}\wedge\,\wedge_{i=1}^{n-1}\partial_{i}F_{u}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\big[\big(-u\,\varphi\,\omega+\varphi\,\nu_{H})+\sigma\,{\rm O}\big(\psi^{2}(u^{2}+v^{2})\big)\big]\cdot

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\cdot\big[A\,C^{n-1}\,\nu_{H}-B\,C^{n-1}\,\omega+\sigma\,{\rm O}\big(P(u)^{2}\big)\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!C^{n-1}\!\big[\big(1-\frac{u^{2}}{2}-u\,r\,\partial_{r}u\big)\,\varphi+(u+r\,\partial_{r}u)\,u\,\varphi\big]\!\!+\sigma\,{\rm O}\big(\psi^{2}\,(w^{2}+P(u)^{2})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\varphi+\sigma\,{\rm O}\big(\psi^{2}\,(w^{2}+P(u)^{2})\big)

In particular, since
|h|\leq\Lambda
,

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\frac{d}{dt}\Big|_{t=0}\,\mathcal{H}^{n}(\Sigma(u+t\varphi,r_{1},r_{2}))=\int_{\Sigma\times(r_{1},r_{2})}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!h\,\,\dot{\Phi}_{0}\big(F_{u}\big)\cdot\star\big(\partial_{r}F_{u}\wedge\,\wedge_{i=1}^{n-1}\partial_{i}F_{u}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\int_{{\Sigma_{H}}\times(r_{1},r_{2})}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!r^{n}\,\psi^{2}w\,h+\sigma\Lambda r_{2}{\rm O}\big(Q_{\psi}(u)+Q_{\psi}(w)+Q_{\psi}(\nabla^{\Sigma_{H}}u)+Q_{r\,\psi}(\partial_{r}u)\big)\,.

Plugging this estimate into (
A.8
), and by
\max\{1,\Lambda\,r_{2}\}\,\sigma\leq\sigma_{0}
, we find (
A.7
).

Step two:
We prove that

\displaystyle Q_{\psi}(\nabla^{\Sigma_{H}}u)+Q_{r\,\psi}(\partial_{r}u)\leq Q_{\psi}(|u|,\Lambda\,r)+C(n)\,\big(Q_{\psi}(u)+Q_{r\,\psi^{\prime}}(u)\big)\,.

(A.9)

By
Q_{r}(\psi\,\partial_{r}u,\psi^{\prime}u)\leq Q_{r\,\psi}(\partial_{r}u)/4+C\,Q_{r\,\psi^{\prime}}(u)
and by (
A.7
)
w=u
we find

\displaystyle Q_{\psi}(\nabla^{\Sigma_{H}}u)+Q_{r\,\psi}(\partial_{r}u)\leq Q_{\psi}(|u|,\Lambda\,r)+C(n)\,\big(Q_{\psi}(u)+Q_{r\,\psi^{\prime}}(u)\big)

\displaystyle+C(n)\,\sigma_{0}\,\big(Q_{\psi}(u)+Q_{r\,\psi^{\prime}}(u)+Q_{\psi}(\nabla^{\Sigma_{H}}u)+Q_{r\,\psi}(\partial_{r}u)\big)\,.

which implies (
A.9
) provided
\sigma_{0}
is small enough.

Step three:
We prove that, if
w:\Sigma_{H}\times(r_{1},r_{2})\to\mathbb{R}
is slice-wise orthogonal to
u-w
, in the sense that
\int_{\Sigma_{H}}w_{r}\,(u_{r}-w_{r})=0
,
\int_{\Sigma_{H}}\partial_{r}w_{r}\,(\partial_{r}u_{r}-\partial_{r}w_{r})=0
, and
\int_{\Sigma_{H}}\nabla^{\Sigma_{H}}w_{r}\cdot(\nabla^{\Sigma_{H}}u_{r}-\nabla^{\Sigma_{H}}w_{r})=0
for every
r\in(r_{1},r_{2})
, then

|T_{\psi}(u,w)|\leq Q_{\psi}(|w|,\Lambda\,r)+C(n)\,\sigma_{0}\,\big(Q_{\psi}(u)+Q_{r\,\psi^{\prime}}(u)+Q_{\psi}(|u|,\Lambda\,r)\big)\,.

(A.10)

Indeed, by slice-wise orthogonality, we find that
Q_{\zeta}(w)\leq Q_{\zeta}(u)
,
Q_{\zeta}(\partial_{r}w)\leq Q_{\zeta}(\partial_{r}u)
and
Q_{\zeta}(\nabla^{\Sigma_{H}}w)\leq Q_{\zeta}(\nabla^{\Sigma_{H}}u)
whenever
\zeta:(r_{1},r_{2})\to\mathbb{R}
is radial. Therefore (
A.7
) gives

|T_{\psi}(u,w)|\leq Q_{\psi}(|w|,\Lambda\,r)+C(n)\,\sigma_{0}\,R_{\psi}(u)
, with
R_{\psi}(u)=Q_{\psi}(u)+Q_{\psi}(\nabla^{\Sigma_{H}}u)+Q_{r\,\psi}(\partial_{r}u)+Q_{r\,\psi^{\prime}}(u)
. Combining this with (
A.9
) we get (
A.10
).

Step four:
We prove (
A.1
). Let now
\psi
be a cut-off function between
(r_{3},r_{4})
and
(r_{1},r_{2})
, so that with
Z_{\psi}(u)=Q_{\psi}(\nabla^{\Sigma_{H}}u)+Q_{\psi}(u)+Q_{r\,\psi}(\partial_{r}u)
,

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\Sigma_{H}\times(r_{3},r_{4})}r^{n-1}\big\{|\nabla^{\Sigma_{H}}u|^{2}-(n-1)\,u^{2}+(r\,\partial_{r}u)^{2}\big\}\Big|\leq Z_{\psi}(u)\,.

If
A(u)=\mathcal{H}^{n}(\Sigma_{H}(u,r_{3},r_{4}))-\mathcal{H}^{n}(\Sigma_{H}(0,r_{3},r_{4}))
, then by (
A.3
) with
(r_{3},r_{4})
in place of
(r_{1},r_{2})
, we find

\displaystyle|A(u)|\leq Z_{\psi}(u)+C(n)\,\sigma\,Z_{\psi}(u)\leq Q_{\psi}(|u|,\Lambda\,r)+C(n)\,\big(Q_{\psi}(u)+Q_{r\,\psi^{\prime}}(u)\big)

\displaystyle+C(n)\,\sigma\,\big\{Q_{\psi}(u)+Q_{\psi}(|u|,\Lambda\,r)+C(n)\,\big(Q_{\psi}(u)+Q_{r\,\psi^{\prime}}(u)\big)\big\}\,,

where in the last inequality we have used (
A.9
). We deduce

|A(u)|\leq C(n)\,\big(Q_{\psi}(|u|,\Lambda\,r)+Q_{\psi}(u)+Q_{r\,\psi^{\prime}}(u)\big)\,,

(A.11)

and (
A.1
) follows (with
C_{0}=C_{0}(n,\eta_{0},\eta)
by the properties of
\psi
).

Step five:
We finally prove that, if
E^{0}_{\Sigma_{H}}[u_{r_{*}}]=0
for some
r_{*}\in(r_{1},r_{2})
, then (
A.2
) holds, that is

\int_{\Sigma_{H}\times(r_{3},r_{4})}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!r^{n-1}\,u^{2}\leq C(n)\,\Lambda\,r_{2}\,(r_{2}^{n}-r_{1}^{n})+C(n,\eta_{0},\eta)\,\int_{\Sigma\times(r_{1},r_{2})}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!r^{n-1}\,(r\,\partial_{r}u)^{2}\,.

(A.12)

Define
u^{+},u^{-},u^{0}:\Sigma_{H}\times(r_{1},r_{2})\to\mathbb{R}
by setting, for
r\in(r_{1},r_{2})
,

(u^{+})_{r}=E^{+}_{\Sigma_{H}}[u_{r}]
,
(u^{-})_{r}=E^{-}_{\Sigma_{H}}[u_{r}]
and
(u^{0})_{r}=E^{0}_{\Sigma_{H}}[u_{r}]
,
where
E^{\pm}_{\Sigma_{H}}
denote the
L^{2}(\Sigma_{H})
-orthogonal projections on the spaces of positive/negative eigenvectors of the Jacobi operator of
\Sigma_{H}
, and where
E^{0}_{\Sigma_{H}}
is the
L^{2}(\Sigma_{H})
-orthogonal projection onto the space of the Jacobi fields of
\Sigma_{H}
. Since
(u^{0})_{r_{*}}=0
, we can directly apply (
A.6
) with
v=u^{0}
and deduce that

\int_{\Sigma_{H}\times(r_{1},r_{2})}r^{n-1}\,(u^{0})^{2}\leq C(n,\eta_{0})\,\int_{\Sigma_{H}\times(r_{1},r_{2})}r^{n-1}\,(r\,\partial_{r}u^{0})^{2}\,.

(A.13)

By the orthogonality relations between
u^{0}_{r}
,
u^{+}_{r}
and
u^{-}_{r}
we have that

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\int_{\Sigma_{H}\times(r_{3},r_{4})}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!r^{n-1}\,u^{2}=\int_{\Sigma_{H}\times(a,b)}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!r^{n-1}\,\Big((u^{0})^{2}+(u^{+})^{2}+(u^{-})^{2}\Big)

(A.14)

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\int_{\Sigma_{H}\times(r_{1},r_{2})}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!r^{n+1}\,(\partial_{r}u)^{2}=\int_{\Sigma_{H}\times(r_{1},r_{2})}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!r^{n+1}\,\big((\partial_{r}u^{0})^{2}+(\partial_{r}u^{+})^{2}+(\partial_{r}u^{-})^{2}\big).

(A.15)

By the spectral theorem, for every
r\in(r_{1},r_{2})
we have

C_{1}(n)^{-1}\,\int_{\Sigma_{H}}(u^{-})_{r}^{2}\leq\,\int_{\Sigma_{H}}\,(n-1)\,(u^{-})_{r}^{2}-|\nabla^{\Sigma_{H}}(u^{-})_{r}|^{2}\,,

which, multiplied by
r^{n-1}\,\psi^{2}
, gives

\displaystyle C_{1}(n)^{-1}\,Q_{\psi}(u^{-})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(n-1)\,Q_{\psi}(u^{-})-Q_{\psi}(\nabla^{\Sigma_{H}}u^{-})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(n-1)\,Q_{\psi}(u^{-},u)-Q_{\psi}(\nabla^{\Sigma_{H}}u^{-},\nabla^{\Sigma_{H}}u)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{\psi}(u^{-},u)+Q_{r}(\partial_{r}u,\partial_{r}(\psi^{2}\,u^{-}))\,,

where in the second to last identity we have used that
w=u^{-}
is slice-wise orthogonal to
w-u
; in particular, by (
A.10
) with
w=u^{-}
, we find

\displaystyle C_{1}(n)^{-1}\,Q_{\psi}(u^{-})\leq Q_{\psi}(|u^{-}|,\Lambda\,r)+Q_{r}(\partial_{r}u,\partial_{r}(\psi^{2}\,u^{-}))

\displaystyle+C(n)\,\sigma_{0}\,\big(Q_{\psi}(u)+Q_{r\,\psi^{\prime}}(u)+Q_{\psi}(|u|,\Lambda\,r)\big)\,.

(A.16)

Again by slice-wise orthogonality of
w=u^{-}
to
w-u
, we have

\displaystyle Q_{r}(\partial_{r}u,\partial_{r}(\psi^{2}\,u^{-}))=Q_{r}(\partial_{r}u^{-},\partial_{r}(\psi^{2}\,u^{-}))=Q_{r\,\psi}(\partial_{r}u^{-})

\displaystyle+2\,Q_{r}(\psi^{\prime}\,\partial_{r}u^{-},\psi\,u^{-})\leq Q_{r\,\psi}(\partial_{r}u^{-})+\frac{Q_{\psi}(u^{-})}{2\,C_{1}(n)}+C(n)\,Q_{r\,\psi^{\prime}}(\partial_{r}u^{-})\,,

which combined into (
A.16
) gives

\displaystyle(2\,C_{1}(n))^{-1}\,Q_{\psi}(u^{-})\leq Q_{\psi}(|u^{-}|,\Lambda\,r)+Q_{r\,\psi}(\partial_{r}u^{-})+C(n)\,Q_{r\,\psi^{\prime}}(\partial_{r}u^{-})

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt+C(n)\,\sigma_{0}\,\big(Q_{\psi}(u)+Q_{r\,\psi^{\prime}}(u)+Q_{\psi}(|u|,\Lambda,r)\big)\,.

Using Hölder inequality again we have

\displaystyle Q_{\psi}(|u^{-}|,\Lambda\,r)\leq\frac{Q_{\psi}(u^{-})}{4\,C_{1}(n)}+C(n)\,\Lambda\,r_{2}\,(r_{2}^{n}-r_{1}^{n})\,,

\displaystyle Q_{\psi}(|u|,\Lambda\,r)\leq 2\,Q_{\psi}(u)+C(n)\,\Lambda\,r_{2}\,(r_{2}^{n}-r_{1}^{n})\,,

so that

\displaystyle\frac{Q_{\psi}(u^{-})}{4\,C_{1}(n)}\leq Q_{r\,\psi}(\partial_{r}u^{-})+C(n)\big(Q_{r\,\psi^{\prime}}(\partial_{r}u^{-})+\Lambda\,r_{2}\,(r_{2}^{n}-r_{1}^{n})\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+C(n)\,\sigma_{0}\,\big(Q_{\psi}(u)+Q_{r\,\psi^{\prime}}(u)+\Lambda\,r_{2}\,(r_{2}^{n}-r_{1}^{n})\big)

Taking
\psi
to be a cut-off function between
(r_{3},r_{4})
and
(r_{1},r_{2})
, we find

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma\times(r_{3},r_{4})}\!\!\!\!\!\!r^{n-1}\,(u^{-})^{2}\leq C(n)\,\Lambda\,r_{2}\,(r_{2}^{n}-r_{1}^{n})

(A.17)

\displaystyle+C(n,\eta_{0},\eta)\,\Big\{\int_{\Sigma\times(r_{1},r_{2})}\!\!\!\!\!\!r^{n-1}\,(r\,\partial_{r}u^{-})^{2}+\sigma_{0}\,\int_{\Sigma\times(r_{1},r_{2})}\!\!\!\!\!\!r^{n-1}\,u^{2}\Big\}\,.

By combining (
A.13
), (
A.17
), and the analogous estimate to (
A.17
) for
u^{+}
with (
A.14
) and (
A.15
) we find that (
A.17
) holds with
u
in place of
u^{-}
; this latter estimate,
thanks to (
A.5
),
finally gives (
A.12
).

Appendix B
Spherical and cylindrical graphs

We state here for the reader’s convenience two technical lemmas concerning spherical and cylindrical graphs. They are both used in the last step of the proof of Theorem
1.6
. The elementary proofs are omitted.

Lemma B.1

(Spherical graphs as cylindrical graphs)
.

There are dimension independent positive constants
C
and
\eta_{0}
with the following property. If
n\geq 1
,
H\in\mathcal{H}
and
u\in\mathcal{X}_{\eta}(\Sigma_{H},r_{1},r_{2})
with
\eta<\eta_{0}
, then we have

\textbf{{D}}_{(1-C\,\eta^{2})\,r_{2}}^{\nu_{H}}\setminus\textbf{{D}}_{r_{1}}^{\nu_{H}}\subset\mathbf{p}_{H}\big(\Sigma_{H}(u,r_{1},r_{2})\big)\subset\textbf{{D}}_{r_{2}}^{\nu_{H}}\setminus\textbf{{D}}_{(1-C\,\eta^{2})\,r_{1}}^{\nu_{H}}\,,

and there is
g\in C^{1}(H)
such that
\sup\big\{|x|^{-1}\,|g(x)|+|\nabla g(x)|:x\in H\big\}\leq C\,\eta
and

\Sigma_{H}(u,r_{1},r_{2})=\big\{x+g(x)\,\nu_{H}:x\in\mathbf{p}_{H}\big(\Sigma_{H}(u,r_{1},r_{2})\big)\big\}
.
Moreover, if
(\rho_{1},\rho_{2})\subset((1+C\,\eta)\,r_{1},(1-C\,\eta^{2})\,r_{2})
, then

\Sigma_{H}(u,\rho_{1},\rho_{2})=\big\{x+g(x)\,\nu_{H}:x\in H\big\}\cap A_{\rho_{1}}^{\rho_{2}}
.

Lemma B.2
.

There is
\eta\in(0,1)
with the following property. If
H\in\mathcal{H}
,
R>1
,
f\in C^{2}(H)
, and
g\in C^{1}(H)
are such that

\max\big\{|f(x)|\,,\,\,|x|\,|\nabla f(x)|\,,\,\,|x|\,|\nabla^{2}f(x)|:x\in H\,,|x|>R\big\}<\eta\,,

(B.1)

\max\big\{|x|^{-1}\,|g(x)|,|\nabla g(x)|:x\in H\big\}<\eta\,,

(B.2)

then there is
h\in C^{1}(G_{H}(f))
such that

\displaystyle G_{H}(g)\setminus B_{4\,R}=\big\{z+h(z)\,\nu_{f}(z):z\in G_{H}(f)\big\}\setminus B_{4\,R}\,,

(B.3)

where
G_{H}(f)=\{x+f(x)\,\nu_{H}:x\in H\}
and, for
z=x+f(x)\,\nu_{H}
, we have set
\nu_{f}(z)=(1+|\nabla f(x)|^{2})^{-1/2}\,(-\nabla f(x)+\nu_{H})
.

Appendix C
Obstacles with zero isoperimetric residue

Proposition C.1
.

If
W
is compact and
\mathcal{R}(W)=0
, then
\psi_{W}(v)-P(B^{(v)})\to 0
as
v\to\infty
and
W
is purely
\mathcal{H}^{n}
-unrectifiable, in the sense that
W
cannot contain an
\mathcal{H}^{n}
-rectifiable set of
\mathcal{H}^{n}
-positive measure. In a partial converse, if
W
is purely
\mathcal{H}^{n}
-unrectifiable and
\mathcal{H}^{n}(W)<\infty
, then
\mathcal{R}(W)=0
.

Proof.

Step one:
Let
\mathcal{R}(W)=0
. Comparing with balls,
\varlimsup_{v\to\infty}\psi_{W}(v)-P(B^{(v)})\leq 0=\mathcal{R}(W)
. To prove the matching lower bound, we argue by contradiction and consider
E_{j}\in{\rm Min}[\psi_{W}(v_{j})]
with
v_{j}\to\infty
such that

\varliminf_{v\to\infty}\psi_{W}(v)-P(B^{(v)})=\lim_{j\to\infty}P(E_{j};\Omega)-P(B^{(v_{j})})<0\,.

(C.1)

With (
C.1
) replacing
\mathcal{R}(W)>0
, one can repeat
verbatim
step two-(a) of the proof of Theorem
1.1
; we thus derive the asymptotic expansion for
F
as in step two-(c), which is then the key fact used in step three to derive that

\lim_{j\to\infty}P(E_{j};\Omega)-P(B^{(v_{j})})\geq-\mathrm{res}_{W}(F\cup W,\nu)\geq-\mathcal{R}(W)
;
the latter inequality is of course in contradiction with (
C.1
) if
\mathcal{R}(W)=0
. Next, arguing again by contradiction, we assume the existence of an
\mathcal{H}^{n}
-rectifiable set
S
with
\mathcal{H}^{n}(W\cap S)>0
. By
[
Sim83b
, Lemma 11.1]
, without loss of generality,
S
is a
C^{1}
-embedded hypersurface in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
. Let
x
be a point of tangential differentiability for
W\cap S
, so that
\mathcal{H}^{n}(W\cap S\cap B_{\rho}(x))=\omega_{n}\,\rho^{n}+{\rm o}_{x}(\rho^{n})
as
\rho\to 0^{+}
. Since
S
is a
C^{1}
-embedded hypersurface, there is
\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{n}
such that for every
\varepsilon>0
there is
\rho_{*}=\rho_{*}(x,\varepsilon)>0
with

S\cap\textbf{{C}}_{\rho_{*},\rho_{*}}^{\nu}(x)=\{y+g(y)\,\nu:y\in\textbf{{D}}_{\rho_{*}}^{\nu}(x)\}
, where
g\in C^{1}(x+\nu^{\perp})
with
g(x)=0
and
{\rm Lip}(g)\leq\varepsilon
. Denoting
G(g)=\{y+g(y)\,\nu:y\in(x+\nu^{\perp})\}
, and up to decrease
\rho_{*}
, we can entail

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{n}\big(G(g)\cap W\cap\textbf{{C}}_{\rho_{*}}^{\nu}(x)\big)\geq\mathcal{H}^{n}(W\cap S\cap B_{\rho_{*}}(x))\geq(1-\varepsilon)\,\omega_{n}\,\rho_{*}^{n}\,.

(C.2)

Since
|g|\leq\varepsilon\,\rho_{*}
on
\partial\textbf{{D}}_{\rho_{*}}^{\nu}(x)
, we can define
f:(x+\nu^{\perp})\to\mathbb{R}
so that
f=g
on
\textbf{{D}}_{\rho_{*}}^{\nu}(x)
,
f=0
on
(x+\nu^{\perp})\setminus\textbf{{D}}_{2\,\rho_{*}}^{\nu}(x)
, and
{\rm Lip}(f)\leq\varepsilon
. Denoting by
F
the epigraph of
f
, we have that
(F,\nu)\in\mathcal{F}
and we compute, for
R
large enough to entail
\textbf{{C}}_{2\,\rho_{*}}^{\nu}(x)\cup W\subset\subset\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu}
,

\displaystyle\omega_{n}\,R^{n}-P(F;\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu}\setminus W)\!\!

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\!\!\omega_{n}\,(2{\rho_{*}})^{n}-P(F;\textbf{{C}}_{2\,{\rho_{*}}}^{\nu}(x)\setminus W)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\int_{\textbf{{D}}_{2\,{\rho_{*}}}^{\nu}(x)}\!\!\!1-\sqrt{1+|\nabla f|^{2}}+P(F;\textbf{{C}}_{2\,{\rho_{*}}}^{\nu}(x)\cap W)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\!\!-\omega_{n}\,(2\,{\rho_{*}})^{n}\,\varepsilon^{2}+(1-\varepsilon)\,\omega_{n}\,{\rho_{*}}^{n}

where we have used
f=0
on
\nu^{\perp}\setminus\textbf{{D}}_{2\,{\rho_{*}}}^{\nu}(x)
, (
C.2
) and
\sqrt{1+\varepsilon^{2}}\leq 1+\varepsilon^{2}
. Up to taking
\varepsilon<\varepsilon(n)
, we thus find
{\rm res}_{W}(F,\nu)>0
, and thus deduce
\mathcal{R}(W)>0
.

Step two:
Let
W
be purely
\mathcal{H}^{n}
-unrectifiable with
\mathcal{H}^{n}(W)<\infty
, and let
(F,\nu)\in{\rm Max}[\mathcal{R}(W)]
. Since
F
is a local perimeter minimizer in
\Omega
,
F
is open in
\Omega
with
\Omega\cap\partial F=\mathrm{cl}\,(\partial^{*}F)
(
\partial^{*}F=
the reduced boundary of
F
as a set of locally finite perimeter in
\Omega
). Now,
\omega_{n}\,R^{n}-P(F;\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu}\setminus W)
is decreasing towards
\mathcal{R}(W)\geq\mathcal{S}(W)\geq 0
, therefore
P(F;\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu}\setminus W)<\infty
for every
R
. In particular,
\mathcal{H}^{n}\llcorner(\Omega\cap\partial F)
is a Radon measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
. Now,
\partial F\subset(\Omega\cap\partial F)\cup W
, so that
\mathcal{H}^{n}(W)<\infty
implies that
\mathcal{H}^{n}\llcorner\partial F
is a Radon measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
and, since
F
is open, that
F
is a set of finite perimeter in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
by
[
Fed69
, Theorem 4.5.11]
. The pure
\mathcal{H}^{n}
-unrectifiability of
W
gives
P(F;\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu}\setminus W)=P(F;\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu})
, where
P(F;\textbf{{C}}_{R}^{\nu})\geq\omega_{n}\,R^{n}
by (
1.8
) and (
1.9
), and thus
\mathcal{R}(W)={\rm res}_{W}(F,\nu)\leq 0
. This proves
\mathcal{R}(W)=0
.
∎
